# Wiki Log

Append one entry after every scaffold change, version lifecycle event, ingest, trace, filed answer, measurement run, or standalone lint run the user asked for. Record the lint result in the entry, and run lint after writing it.

## [2026-10-08] file v17 | EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, SETTINGS) tutorial with bloated-index scenarios

- Request and prompt hygiene: the prompt read "follow  agents.md, in postgresql 17, question : create a comprehensive tutorial on how to read `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, SETTINGS)`, how to use it to find issue on plan when indexes are missing or not in use because index bloat , use the  common-concept: mandatory-btree-bloat-test.md to generate test scenarios with bloated indexes." Corrected, and restated in the page's `## Question`: "Follow AGENTS.md. In PostgreSQL 17, question: create a comprehensive tutorial on how to read EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, SETTINGS) and how to use it to find issues in plans when indexes are missing or not in use because of index bloat. Use the common concept mandatory-btree-bloat-tests.md to generate test scenarios with bloated indexes." Corrections: collapsed the double spaces; capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md and PostgreSQL; "question :" to "question:"; joined the two "how to" clauses with "and"; "find issue on plan" to "find issues in plans"; "because index bloat ," to "because of index bloat."; "use the common-concept:" to "Use the common concept"; the file name `mandatory-btree-bloat-test.md` to the existing `mandatory-btree-bloat-tests.md`, the only page it can mean; and the inline code marks dropped, because the restatement sits in a text block.
- Filed: [Reading EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, SETTINGS) to Find Missing, Unusable and Bloated Indexes in PostgreSQL 17 (unverified)](v17/questions/observability/explain-analyze-buffers-settings-tutorial.md), the first v17 page in the `observability` category ("EXPLAIN instrumentation output"), at pin `786db8dcf168bd9df8f55047337525ac19118b1c`. Evidence came from the pinned checkout only: EXPLAIN option parsing, printing and `SETTINGS`; executor instrumentation and parallel aggregation; buffer-manager counters; `GUC_EXPLAIN`; planner relation, index and cost code; B-tree search, deletion and build fillfactor; VACUUM's cleanup-lock path; `pgstattuple`; the docs; and `explain.sql`. The page has 351 citations into 48 files, 28 tables, 13 text diagrams and plan excerpts, 30 Evidence Map rows and 7 Open Questions bullets; after drafting, every one of its 198 unique cited ranges was opened and matched to its label, which corrected two ranges, the `GUC_EXPLAIN` count (59 in a standard build, not 60) and one operator-family claim that now cites `pg_amop.dat`.
- Rule precedence: MANDATORY Production SQL asks for session-scoped `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, while the Mandatory B-Tree Bloat Tests protocol the user asked for forbids a timeout that could fire inside the maintenance `VACUUM`. Followed the user's instruction (precedence 1): maintenance sessions run with all four settable timeouts at 0, as an autovacuum worker forces them, and every other session with `statement_timeout = 30min` and `lock_timeout = 60s`. The page names both.
- Measurement: one staged Bash and SQL script (`build check cluster harness controls fixtures baseline churn decide oracle coldread report`, plus `stop` and `clean`) in sandbox `.wiki-runtime/tmp/exbt/`, port 55437, `autovacuum = off`. Controls on `orders` and `customers` cover a missing index, cast and `numeric`-literal mismatches, an invalid index left by a failed unique `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, disabled methods, `random_page_cost` 1000 and 1.1, low selectivity, a nested loop, a sort spill, serial and parallel lossy bitmaps, a never-executed subtree, and JSON and plain `EXPLAIN`. Three B-tree fixtures went through the protocol's five phases: `drand` (key uncorrelated with the heap) and `dseq` (correlated) under rule 2's uniform drain, and `ffctl` (fillfactor 50, drain-exempt). Proofs: 0 dead-but-not-removable, 0 missed, no horizon holders, no skip lines, all timeouts 0; rule 3's census analyzed nothing; the oracle gave back 89.95 %, 89.95 % and 0.00 %. Three runs were made: a trial on the prototype build; a full run whose `dseq` maintenance missed one heap page (136 tuples) to cleanup-lock contention, which led to the added `CHECKPOINT` and missed-tuple proof; and the filed run, 2026-10-08 15:25:49Z to 15:27:03Z, from a fresh build, with `make check` All 225 and `pgstattuple` All 1. The published script is byte-identical to the text that ran (md5 `e202aff678bed8e23425c47d19d730f3`).
- Glossary: added [EXPLAIN SETTINGS](glossary.md#explain-settings), [Plan node instrumentation](glossary.md#plan-node-instrumentation) and [seq_page_cost and random_page_cost](glossary.md#seq_page_cost-and-random_page_cost), checked on PostgreSQL 17 only, with Contents and Scope updated; the PostgreSQL 17 Source References group went from 522 to 525 files, and EXPLAIN BUFFERS gained two `Related:` links. Review of the existing entries the page leans on (EXPLAIN, EXPLAIN BUFFERS, Ring buffer, Bulk writer, OS page cache, Leaf page, B-tree page deletion, Invalid index, Selectivity, Fast root, IndexOptInfo, Index-only scan, Fillfactor, pgstatindex): no defect found. ## [2026-10-08] review and fix v12 | Database health checklist: all findings fixed, measurement script published and run, two glossary entries

- Request and prompt hygiene: the request read "follow  agents.md, in postgresql 12, review question:  PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist". Corrected: "Follow AGENTS.md. In PostgreSQL 12, review the question 'PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist'." Corrections: collapsed the two double spaces; capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, In and PostgreSQL; added "the"; split the request into sentences and quoted the title. The scope answer, a selected option, was "Fix and re-run". The page's `## Question` text was not touched; the 2026-06-26 filing entry never recorded that prompt's original wording, so it cannot be recovered.
- Rule precedence: the request named no scope, so the findings were reported before any edit and one scope question followed (MANDATORY Review Requests over the same-task glossary and bookkeeping rules); glossary maintenance and this entry waited for the answer. The measurement script calls `pgrep` and `lsof`, which are outside the POSIX tool list of MANDATORY Measurement Script, because MANDATORY Environment Isolation requires the process and port teardown checks they perform (precedence 2 over document-shape rules); the page names both tools and why.
- Review: target [PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist (unverified)](v12/questions/server-administration/database-health-checklist.md) at unchanged pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42` (12.2). Two statements contradicted the pin: the subscription query joined on `pg_subscription.oid`, which PUBLIC cannot read, so it failed for every non-superuser including `pg_monitor`; and the checkpoint text said only the frequency message reveals WAL-caused checkpoints and called it a warning, while `log_checkpoints` start lines carry a ` wal` flag, the message is LOG severity, and restartpoints never emit it. Missing PostgreSQL 12 behavior included the recovery-time statistics wipe, `canceling autovacuum task`, the unremovable-row fields of the autovacuum summary, extended-protocol duration lines, the `invalid page` and `zeroing out page` messages, the member-space and `max_wal_senders` messages, catalog maintenance and temporary-table limits. Five citation defects and the AGENTS.md rules added after the July revision (Non-Default Behavior, GUC Changes, Measurement Script, Shared Glossary, Technical Explanations) were also reported.
- Fix: rewrote the Answer around three new mental-model tables (What each source of evidence represents, Who can see what, When counters reset) and a defaults table (Defaults that decide what you see), then corrected the checklist and log table. The subscription query became two `pg_monitor`-safe queries joined by name, with the duplicate-name limit stated. Added the catalog-maintenance query, the `hidden_backend_type` capacity column, log rows for extended-protocol durations, autovacuum cancellation, recovery-conflict ERROR versus FATAL, the crash and reinitialization chain, damaged pages, archive failures and the `max_wal_senders` FATAL, and a slot-retention item. Fixed the five citation defects (`pg_locks.pid`, the `basebackup.c` range, stale `stats.sql` and `autovacuum.c` Source References, the `wal_level` default, the `pg_stat_clear_snapshot` test). The page now has 561 citations into 74 files, links 52 glossary terms and the glossary in Navigation, and gained two Open Questions (the `numbackends` doc-versus-view mismatch and the standby's early time-flagged restartpoint); the existing progress-gate question now notes that the script measured the gate. Source-only finding added to the lifecycle table: an immediate shutdown is not `FatalError` to the postmaster, so `pg_stat_statements` dumps and survives it, while a backend crash discards its entries; the collector's counters are wiped in both cases.
- Measurement: one staged Bash and SQL script (`build default cluster standby access prepared deadlock logs autovac conflict crash checksum sqlcheck report`, plus `stop` and `clean`) in sandbox `.wiki-runtime/tmp/dh12/`, ports 55471-55473: a primary with documented non-default logging and 2PC settings, a slot-backed streaming standby, and an `initdb -k` checksum cluster. Trial runs exposed four script defects, all fixed before the filed run: an assertion expected `t` where `text || boolean` renders `true`; with `max_wal_size = 32MB` and no slot, the primary removed segment `000000010000000000000003` while the standby still needed it, so the standby now uses a physical slot; a crash marker shared a `queryid` with an earlier statement of the same shape, so markers now reference a fixture table; and the single immediate-shutdown crash leg could not show `pg_stat_statements` loss, so a `SIGKILL` leg was added. Filed run: from an empty sandbox, cold build 61 s with `DH12_JOBS=16`, measurement stages 61 s, report at 2026-10-08 19:52:57Z, every built-in check passed. The published script is byte-identical to the text that ran (md5 `57ab4b1be577f38bf09fc7d0db97be7a`), and the 11 SQL blocks are unchanged since `sqlcheck` ran them (cksum `1515629033 13440`).
- Glossary: added [Default roles](glossary.md#default-roles) and [WAL archiving](glossary.md#wal-archiving), checked on PostgreSQL 12 only, and PostgreSQL 12 notes for [Temporary file](glossary.md#temporary-file) (holds, two named exceptions), [Virtual transaction ID](glossary.md#virtual-transaction-id) (differs: `BackendId`, and no follow-through to a prepared transaction's XID) and [transaction_timeout and idle_in_transaction_session_timeout](glossary.md#transaction_timeout-and-idle_in_transaction_session_timeout) (differs: no `transaction_timeout`). Contents, Scope and the PostgreSQL 12 Source References group were updated; that group went from 363 to 377 files, one of them `joinpath.c`, a pre-existing gap. The glossary has 334 terms and keeps `verified_by_agent: not yet`. Base backup was not linked from the page, so its missing 12 note was left for a later task. - Navigation: rewrote the page's blurbs in `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md`; the six glossary blurbs say 334 terms; `wiki/versions.md` has a dated coverage note. No common concept page was created or changed; PostgreSQL 12 has none, and pages on cumulative statistics and transaction ID wraparound remain proposals.

## [2026-10-13] concept v17 | Recommended VACUUM completeness procedure on the three bloat-test common concept pages

- Request and prompt hygiene: the request read "follow agents.md, in postgresql 17, update all common-concepts propose a way to tester to verify or garantee that vacuum did all its job no features were skipped due to locking or another transaction runnning or any other thing that could prevent vacuum from running properly." Corrections: capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, PostgreSQL and VACUUM; "common-concepts" became "common concepts"; inserted "to" before "propose"; "a way to tester" became "a way for testers"; "garantee" became "guarantee"; "no features were skipped" became "that no feature was skipped"; "another transaction runnning" became "another running transaction"; "due to" became "because of"; sentence punctuation added. No correction changed the meaning.
- Scope and rule precedence: PostgreSQL 17 has three common concept pages, all bloat-test protocols, and all three were updated. Asked through the question tool, the user chose a recommended rather than mandatory procedure, tiered escalation (re-run on a transient skip, escalate only when the re-run fails the same way), and coverage of every `VACUUM` feature. No rule conflicted with the request. The binding `The maintenance must not be defeated` rule and its four proofs were left as they were.
- Added on each page, after `The maintenance must not be defeated`: `### Recommended: proving VACUUM did all its work`. One shared core: a value table; a logic map of ten nodes (relation gate, removal cutoff, heap pass with the cleanup-lock fallback, index vacuuming with `INDEX_CLEANUP`, the bypass and the failsafe, index cleanup, truncation, statistics, TOAST, `ANALYZE`, `datfrozenxid`) with a keyed evidence table; Stage A gate checks with SQL (horizon holders, other sessions, relation locks, `CHECKPOINT`, storage parameters, failsafe distance, statistics publication, timeouts, counters); Stage B's tier-0 statement `VACUUM (VERBOSE, ANALYZE, PARALLEL 0)`, serial because an autovacuum worker never vacuums in parallel; Stage C's line-by-line report table with POSIX checks; Stage D's statistics and visibility-map cross-check; Stage E's tiered escalation with the declared `FREEZE` and `INDEX_CLEANUP ON` tiers in default, cause and mechanism form; by-design behaviors; a lifecycle table; and why the `VACUUM` and `ANALYZE` dead-row counters disagree on purpose. One index half per page: B-tree (leaf cleanup-lock waits, the 5 % cleanup-scan rule, the one-`VACUUM` recycling lag), GIN (pending list, posting-tree waits, the census that runs either way) and hash, GiST, SP-GiST and BRIN (conditional squeeze, page-deletion give-ups, redirect horizon, partial range).
- Also on each page: a Definition sentence, a pointer after the four proofs naming what they miss (a refused cleanup lock and the bypass both leave `dead but not yet removable` at 0), rows in Where It Appears, Callers and callees, Shipped tests (the `vacuum-no-cleanup-lock`, `horizons` and `vacuum-skip-locked` specs, `vacuum.sql`, and the bypass's absence), Related Structures, Interactions, Context Reviewed and Evidence Map, five new Open Questions, and every newly cited range in Source References (the B-tree page's seven earlier gaps included). - Request and prompt hygiene: the request read "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12, review question: # PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist (unverified)". Corrected: "Follow AGENTS.md. In PostgreSQL 12, review the question 'PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist (unverified)'." The scope answer, a selected option, was "Fix and re-run". The page's `## Question` text was not touched.
- Rule precedence: the request named no scope, so the findings were reported before any edit and one scope question followed (MANDATORY Review Requests, carrying out the user's instruction at precedence 1, over the same-task glossary and bookkeeping rules at precedence 4); glossary maintenance and this entry waited for the answer. The script still calls `pgrep` and `lsof`, which are outside the POSIX tool list of MANDATORY Measurement Script, because MANDATORY Environment Isolation requires the teardown checks they perform (precedence 2); it now refuses to run without them.
- Review: target [PostgreSQL 12 Database Health Checklist (unverified)](v12/questions/server-administration/database-health-checklist.md) at unchanged pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42` (12.2). Static checks passed: 561 citations into 74 files resolved in range, the Contents, front matter and Navigation were correct, all 52 glossary links resolved to entries checked on 12, every stated GUC context and boot value matched `guc.c` and `pg_stat_statements.c`, and the published script and SQL blocks matched the 2026-10-08 run (md5 `57ab4b1b…`, cksum `1515629033 13440`). Eight statements were contradicted or overstated by the pin: ` wal` was said to mean WAL reached `max_wal_size` (the request fires at `max_wal_size` ÷ (1 + `checkpoint_completion_target`)); comments were said never to separate `pg_stat_statements` entries (utility statements are hashed by text); the recovery-conflict FATAL was tied to idle sessions only (subtransactions and dropped databases also end in FATAL); `backend_xmin` was given as the feedback horizon even with a slot (it is NULL there); "without a slot, nothing keeps WAL"; the lock views were given the per-transaction snapshot rule; the stop-limit fix omitted single-user mode and gave an imprecise privilege reason; and `track_io_timing` was said to time every data-file I/O. There were two smaller slips, on `hidden_backend_type` and the `checkpoint_warning` row. Eight PostgreSQL 12 behaviors were reported missing, and six AGENTS.md items needed work: four single-line `#Symbol` labels, two files missing from Source References, unlinked glossary terms, compressed decision logic, the standby's inherited settings left unstated, the `lsof` silent pass, the Runtime row, and an Open Question the source answers.
- Withdrawn: one reported gap, that the lock-wait list misses `process ... failed to acquire ...`, was dropped during the fix. `ProcSleep()` prints that line only when another process kicks the waiter off the lock, and its own comment says the deadlock checker always kicks its own process, so PostgreSQL 12 cannot reach it ([proc.c:1498-1517](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/proc.c#L1498-L1517)). The citation range widened for it was restored. So seven of the eight reported gaps were filled.
- Fix: the eight statements and two slips were corrected in place, with a worked `CheckPointSegments` calculation in section 6, a three-condition decision table for the frequency message, and a seven-row recovery-conflict decision table in section 3. Added data-file fsync PANICs (`data_sync_retry`), every crash-line form and startup-process failure, the `track_counts` gates and their effect on the checksum, deadlock, temp-file and conflict counters, autovacuum's blindness after `pg_stat_reset()` or a single-table reset, the other duration-line settings (`log_duration`, `log_statement`, `log_transaction_sample_rate`), the 150,000,000-XID threshold of `oldest xmin is far in the past` as a worked calculation, and the other causes of `canceling autovacuum task`. The defaults table gained seven rows and a context-to-scope sentence, the observability query six settings, and the blocker section an autocommit rule. The four labels became `file:line`, nine files joined Source References, and seventeen glossary links were added on first use. The page now has 699 citations into 81 files and 66 glossary links. Open Questions: the restartpoint question was answered and removed; two doc-versus-source questions were added (`backend_xmin` with a slot, and `wal.sgml`'s restartpoint trigger). Context Reviewed and the Evidence Map gained the new areas.
- Measurement: the script was edited in place. It now checks for `pgrep` and `lsof` before any stage; `start_cluster` takes extra server options; the `default` stage reads seven more settings; the `standby` stage records the settings the standby took from the copied `dh12.conf`; the `conflict` stage asserts the idle session's reconnect HINT; and a new `restartpoint` stage, between `conflict` and `crash`, restarts the standby twice with `log_min_messages = debug2` (inherited 32 MB, then `max_wal_size = 1GB` on its command line) while the primary runs with `max_wal_size = 1GB` through `ALTER SYSTEM`, reset at the end of the stage. A trial run from an empty sandbox passed every check, and `clean` removed it. Filed run from an empty sandbox, `DH12_JOBS=16`: 18:03:01Z to 18:06:00Z, build 68 s and measurement stages 111 s, every check passed. The restartpoint probe logged a skip and then `restartpoint starting: time` 15,009 ms later, and nothing in the 30 s control window. The `logs` stage gave three `checkpoint starting: wal` and four frequency lines, the last with no start line, which matches `CreateCheckPoint()`'s idle skip. A warm re-run with the build present (18:06:55Z to 18:08:48Z, 113 s, `build` 3 s) also passed; it differed only in timing-dependent counts (three frequency lines, a 15,017 ms gap). - Fix, by finding:

  2. Disabled methods (5.11 item 2 and 5.1): penalty versus gate, with `enable_hashjoin`'s default and full-join exception, and the `disable_cost` uses that need no switch (`WHERE CURRENT OF`, the inner-MCV hash-bucket rule).
  3. Refresh (5.11 item 6): a stored plan keeps its estimates and choices, while execution reads `work_mem`, hash memory, `jit` and the table's current size live, and plan-cache revalidation never checks `work_mem`.
  4. Outer-join reduction: a new comparison table in chapter 3 Step 7 of what each intervening join passes down (`reduce_outer_joins_pass2()`), with an example; the 3.5 eligibility row, the 3.3 logic map, the coverage-matrix row and a 3.10 test gap.
  5. DISTINCT: the 10.3 logic map now shows the no-sorted-path exception and its penalty; 10.4 Step 7 says the exception arises only for unsortable keys and adds the parallel hard gate; the Step 7 switch table names the parallel gate; 10.10 records the test gap.
  6. Gather Merge rows: scoped by builder in 12.4 Step 12.6, the 12.6 table, the Evidence Map and the Open Question. E12.4's 500,000 is attributed to `create_ordered_paths()`, the only branch an ORDER BY query without grouping reaches, and the Open Question now cites the `generate_gather_paths()` header comment.
  7. SQL text (1.1): the planner and `planner_hook` receive `query_string`, the core planner only passes it on, and `pg_stat_statements` stores it.
  8. Overrides (5.2): the forced-setting sentence is tied to the page's own definition, and a new paragraph names the postmaster-command-line, session-startup and `initdb` overrides with their scopes.

## [2026-10-17] scaffold | Mandatory contradiction checks for reviews, modifications and new documents

- Request and prompt hygiene: the request read "add mandatory rule to agents.md  that reviews, mondifications and new documents should be check for contradictons"; the same message also carried it once without "to agents.md". Corrected: "Add a mandatory rule to AGENTS.md that reviews, modifications and new documents should be checked for contradictions." Corrections: capitalized Add and AGENTS.md; added "a"; collapsed the double space; "mondifications" became "modifications"; "be check" became "be checked"; "contradictons" became "contradictions"; added the final period. No correction changed the meaning.
- Change: added `MANDATORY Contradiction Checks` to `AGENTS.md`, right after `MANDATORY Review Requests`, because it governs the same reviews and edits. It defines a contradiction as two statements that cannot both be true for the same version, conditions and state, or a statement the pinned source shows to be false. The scope depends on the work: the whole page for a new document, a substantial revision or a review (or the part the user names, with its copies and dependents); for a modification, each changed statement plus every place that states, summarizes or depends on it; for a change to `AGENTS.md`, `README.md` or a template, the other rules, the templates, `README.md` and what lint actually checks. The section lists the comparison set, typical contradictions, a resolution table (source wins; docs or tests against source and measurements against source keep their existing `## Open Questions` handling; unsettled disagreements go to `## Open Questions`; condition-dependent statements name their condition) and a location table that keeps the existing edit boundaries: fix the document under work, the glossary and the page's own navigation entries; never edit a common concept page during other work; report other pages and pre-existing rule-document conflicts. Method bullets cover the claim inventory, search-then-read, finding every copy of a corrected statement, the orchestrator's check of an assembled page when work is split, the verbatim Question, dated records, and the `verified_by_agent` timestamp. The outcome goes in the log entry, or in the response when there is none.
- Cross-references: a bullet in `MANDATORY Review Requests`; a new step 12 in `MANDATORY Answer And File` (the old step 12 is now 13); a new step 8 in `MANDATORY Add A Supported Version` (the old step 8 is now 9); a first sentence in step 6 of `MANDATORY File Or Change A Common Concept Document`; a bullet in `MANDATORY Bookkeeping`; a sentence in `MANDATORY Lint`. `MANDATORY Rule Precedence` is unchanged: the rule acts only within the user's authorized scope (item 1) and widens no edit boundary.
- Beyond the literal request, called out so any of them can be dropped: rule-document changes are in scope; dated records and the verbatim Question are never rewritten to remove a contradiction; the orchestrator, not a per-section subagent, owns the cross-section check; a request to fix a reviewed page does not cover other documents.
- Motivation, from this log: on 2026-10-16 the REINDEX CONCURRENTLY failure table contradicted later prose on the same page; on 2026-10-17 four of the planner tutorial's eight findings also contradicted another section, and seven copies needed fixing; on 2026-10-14 the B-tree heuristic page held eight internal contradictions; on 2026-10-08 the v17 index blurb said "115 numbered" while the concept page said 113.

Every `MANDATORY` name cited in `AGENTS.md` resolves to a heading. Five conflicts in the draft were resolved before filing: (1) a full-page check for every review would have overridden a review the user limits, against Rule Precedence item 1; (2) "the page's entries in `wiki/versions.md`" reached the dated Coverage Notes the rule protects, so it now names the version's row; (3) "correct every copy" contradicted the location table's limits, so copies the task may not edit are reported; (4) "report it in the log entry" assumed every task has one; (5) Review Requests' "fix every reported finding" could have been read to cover a contradiction in another document, so the location table now excludes it and the report lists such items separately. Capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, In and PostgreSQL; replaced the comma splice with sentences; changed "on question" to "in the question page"; dropped the duplicated "(line NNNN)" labels and extra spaces. The follow-up "fix all of 2." was normalized as "Fix all of option 2." The tutorial's original Question is unchanged.
- Scope and rule precedence: the request limited the check to two statements and named no fix scope, so under MANDATORY Review Requests the findings were reported with no edit, followed by one scope question. The answer, "fix all of 2", authorized fixing line 5975 and the two precision issues in the §9.6 table, without a measurement re-run; no number depends on the changed rows. - Evidence: PostgreSQL 17 at `786db8dcf168bd9df8f55047337525ac19118b1c`. `examine_simple_variable()` raises `could not find plan for CTE` or `no plan was made for CTE` when a non-recursive CTE reference has no plan (selfuncs.c:5496-5502), as `set_cte_pathlist()` does (allpaths.c:2899-2905). The `subroot == NULL` punt (selfuncs.c:5506-5508) is reachable only for `FROM` subqueries, because a CTE's subroot is the one `SS_process_ctes()` appends with its plan (subselect.c:1035-1042). The only `-1` plan IDs belong to unreferenced SELECT CTEs (subselect.c:900-908) and inlined CTEs, whose references become `RTE_SUBQUERY` (subselect.c:1179-1212). Default estimates for a CTE column come from a recursive self-reference (selfuncs.c:5421-5431) or a whole-row reference (selfuncs.c:5436-5440).
- Fixes: (1) coverage-matrix row "CTE statistics and pathkeys for the outer query (17)" said "Recursive self-reference or unplanned CTE: defaults"; it now says recursive self-reference or whole-row reference gets defaults and a missing CTE plan raises an internal error, citing selfuncs.c:5436-5440 and 5496-5502; its condition now reads "Non-recursive planned CTE, plain column reference". (2) §9.6 row "CTE statistics for a recursive self-reference or appendrel" now separates the recursive CTE self-reference from the appendrel case, which applies to a `FROM` subquery, not a CTE. (3) §9.6 gained a whole-row-reference row, and the CTE-to-earlier-CTE row now limits the statistics benefit to plain column references.

## [2026-10-20] new page v12 | Zeroed relation file: SELECT, VACUUM, and pg_dump behavior

- Request and prompt hygiene: original wording was "follow agents.md, for postgresql 12, question: with data checksum disabled, of a table with thousands of rows so have multiple files on disk , if one of this files are manually set to zero size does a select of all rows will throw an error? does vacuum or pgdump of the table will error out?" Corrections: "agents.md" -> "AGENTS.md" (satisfied by following this file, not restated on the page); "with data checksum disabled" -> "with data checksums disabled"; "of a table with thousands of rows so have multiple files on disk" -> "on a table with thousands of rows that has multiple files on disk"; "if one of this files are manually set" -> "if one of these files is manually set"; "does a select of all rows will throw an error" -> "does a `SELECT` of all rows throw an error"; "does vacuum or pgdump of the table will error out" -> "do `VACUUM` and `pg_dump` of the table error out". None changed the meaning; the corrected form is the page's `## Question`.
- Scope: question page under `wiki/v12/questions/storage-and-vacuum/` plus the recommended "source + measured run" approach the user picked earlier: a staged script run against a real 12.2 build from the pin.
- Build disclosure: the pin's `configure.in` accepts `--with-segsize` only in whole gigabytes (`RELSEG_SIZE` = `1024/blocksize * segsize * 1024` blocks, default 131072 = 1 GB), so a true multi-segment heap would need >1 GB. The script configures out of tree and edits the generated `pg_config.h` to `RELSEG_SIZE 128` (1 MB segments), verifies the define, and records `SHOW segment_size` = `1MB`, `block_size` = 8192, `data_checksums` = off in `out/platform.txt`. The measured code paths branch on "shorter than `RELSEG_SIZE`", never on the value; this is disclosed on the page under "Non-default build knob used for the measurement" per MANDATORY Non-Default Behavior (default stated, cause, mechanism).
- Premise correction recorded on the page: thousands of rows do not produce multiple data files at the default build; multi-segment requires >1 GB. A small table's extra files are its forks (`_fsm`, `_vm`, `_init`), named per `storage.sgml`.
- Measured findings (all legs deterministic on re-run): zeroing any main-fork file makes `mdnblocks` report the relation ending at the first short/missing segment, so `SELECT` of all rows, `VACUUM`, and `pg_dump` (`COPY ... TO stdout`) all succeed and see fewer rows (2,282 of 8,614 after zeroing `.1`; 6,844 after `.3`; 0 after `.0`; `relpages` 484 -> 128 after one `VACUUM`). Index-driven heap fetches are the erroring path: `XX001 could not read block ... read only 0 of 8192 bytes` (md.c:625) into the hole and `XX000 could not open file ".N" (target block ...): previous segment is only 0 blocks` (md.c:1200) past it; `XX000` because `errcode_for_file_access` falls to `ERRCODE_INTERNAL_ERROR` on a stale errno. A warmed backend (segments pre-opened by `EXPLAIN`) computes the stale larger `nblocks` and errors on the seqscan; its own `VACUUM` succeeds via the all-visible page skip and its `pg_class` write flushes the relcache so the next scan sees the shortened relation. `count(id)` on an index-only scan returns all 8,614 without touching the heap. A deleted (not zeroed) first segment is the only universally erroring case (`58P01` everywhere, `pg_dump` exit 1). A zeroed `_fsm`/`_vm` is treated exactly like a missing fork (`smgr_fsm_nblocks`/`smgr_vm_nblocks` gate the reads): no error, self-recreating. An `INSERT` steered by the surviving `_fsm` errors `XX001`; page-sized tuples bypass the FSM's candidates and extend through the hole, re-exposing stale later-segment rows. Truncating under a running server is unreliable erasure: dirty buffered pages are flushed back at block offsets (`mdwrite`), so `CHECKPOINT` regrew the zeroed segment sparse (335,872 bytes) and `_fsm` whole (24,576 bytes).
- Contradiction check: every claim on the page was checked against its cited pin lines, the glossary entries it links (Fork, Free space map, Visibility map, Storage manager, All-zero page, Checkpoint, Relation size functions — all already carry correct PG12 notes, so no glossary edit was needed), the page's own index entries, and the closest same-version page (observability/corruption-log-messages-with-checksums-disabled.md, which this page links for the `zero_damaged_pages`/`missing chunk` surface; the two pages agree — `RBM_ZERO_ON_ERROR` masks invalid headers only, the `XX001`/`read only %d of %d bytes` text matches). The premise correction ("thousands of rows" vs 1 GB segments) is stated explicitly. No contradictions found; the index-only `count(id)` returning 0 on `t_first` is Seq Scan (plan recorded), not a contradiction with the index-only counts on the other legs. Two residual paths filed under Open Questions: the stale-cached fork-size short read (unreproduced because fork pages stay buffer-cached) and the partially-zeroed/sparse page case.
- Glossary: reviewed the nine linked entries; all hold for PG12 as noted; no new term needed ("segment file" is an alias of Storage manager).
- Bookkeeping: page listed in `wiki/v12/index.md` (Storage and Vacuum) and `wiki/index.md`; dated coverage note added to `wiki/versions.md`.
- Environment isolation: all artifacts under `.wiki-runtime/tmp/zerofile-measure/` (build tree, install, datadir, socket, out/). The cluster was stopped with `pg_ctl -m fast -w stop` and the sandbox deleted by the script's `stop` + `clean` stages; teardown confirmed before final lint.
- Version control: no commit or push requested; none made.
- Lint: final run follows this entry.

## [2026-10-21] file and measure v12 | Checking every table, catalogs included, for a zeroed last segment

- Request and prompt hygiene: original wording was `follow agents.md, in postgresql 12, question: how to check for all table in database including the catalog tables if the last segment of the table was zeroed out? , provide a sql to check an arbritary number of last blocks of all tables of the DB.` Corrections: "follow agents.md" is satisfied by following the file and is not restated; "postgresql" -> "PostgreSQL"; "how to check for all table in database" -> "how can I check every table in a database"; "if the last segment of the table was zeroed out? ," -> "for whether its last segment was zeroed out?"; "provide a sql" -> "Provide SQL"; "arbritary" -> "arbitrary"; "of all tables of the DB" -> "of every table in the database". None changed the meaning. The page answers both meanings of "zeroed out", truncation to zero length and overwriting with zeros, and states that reading the last N blocks cannot see the first.
- Mid-task instructions: the user first asked to zero files with `truncate -s 0 filename.txt`. That conflicted with MANDATORY Measurement Script, which allows only POSIX utilities; under MANDATORY Rule Precedence the explicit instruction won, and the conflict was named in the response. The user then asked for the POSIX form `cat /dev/null > filename.txt`, which removed the conflict. The published script uses `cat /dev/null >`; all filed numbers come from runs of that text. A development run with `truncate -s 0` gave identical results.
- Deliverable: filed [Checking Every Table, Catalogs Included, for a Zeroed Last Segment in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)](v12/questions/storage-and-vacuum/zeroed-last-segment-check-all-tables.md) with three production SQL blocks: a core-SQL tail and witness check over every relation with storage (FSM and VM fork lengths and `relpages` against the `mdnblocks()`-equivalent length, segment-layout rules, and the last `tail_blocks` pages read from disk against `PageIsVerified()`'s header test); an orphan-file query for lost `pg_class` rows; and a `pageinspect` B-tree TID witness. Category `storage-and-vacuum`, by rule 1 of MANDATORY Question Categories.
- Evidence: only PostgreSQL 12 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. Traced the md.c segment invariant, `mdnblocks`, `mdtruncate` and `mdunlink`; genfile.c file functions, path checks and revokes; dbsize.c size, path and filenode functions and `RelidByRelfilenode`; FSM geometry and extension (`fsm_internals.h`, `fsm_logical_to_physical`, `fsm_readbuf`, `fsm_extend`, `FreeSpaceMapTruncateRel`); VM geometry, pin and truncate; `RelationTruncate`; every `relpages` writer; VACUUM's per-page FSM records, VM pins and truncation gate; bulk extension and `COPY`'s skip-FSM option; page header layout and `PageIsVerified`; TID and bitmap end-of-relation filters; the relation mapper; `pageinspect` B-tree functions; psql `:{?name}`; and the GUCs the script sets. No pinned test damages relation files.
- Measurement: one Bash/SQL script, published in the page, built 12.2 out of tree with `RELSEG_SIZE` patched to 1024 blocks (8 MB). The non-default knob is explained on the page: 1 GB segments would need tens of gigabytes to reach the FSM and VM thresholds, and the default-size thresholds are derived rather than measured. The script extracts and runs the page's own SQL blocks. Results: every injected truncation was flagged and no healthy catalog was. The FSM witness fired for `t_last`, `t_ins` and `t_vm`, and the VM witness for `t_vm`. `t_small_last` (428 lost pages) showed only `relpages` and, after one VACUUM rewrote `relpages` 3,500 -> 3,072, only the ambiguous layout finding plus the index witness (max referenced block 3,499). `t_zero` showed 20 all-zero tail pages. The legitimate `t_bound` truncation showed the same zero-length-segment layout as the damage. A `ctid` probe of a lost page returned 0 rows without error. Catalog leg: `pg_class` grown to 1,100 blocks over 49,006 tables, segment 1 emptied: the main query flagged `pg_class` (1,024 vs `relpages` 1,100), and the orphan query returned exactly the 3,376 files whose rows were lost. An index-only count still returned 49,006 (`Heap Fetches: 0`), while the forced sequential count returned 45,630. The script now records both counts after that was found. The final text was run twice, from an empty sandbox (127 s) and with the build present (26 s): 24 of 24 assertions both times, identical results, Darwin 27.0.0 arm64, block size 8192, maximum alignment 8, checksums off.
- Glossary: added [Relation mapper](glossary.md#relation-mapper), checked on 12 only, with its Contents entry. Added PostgreSQL 12 notes: bulk extension's uninitialized pages ([All-zero page](glossary.md#all-zero-page)), leaf geometry, extension and truncation ([Free space map](glossary.md#free-space-map)), map-page coverage and VACUUM pins ([Visibility map](glossary.md#visibility-map)), and the inactive-segment layout ([Storage manager](glossary.md#storage-manager)). Added a dated Scope note and two v12 source references (381 files). Reviewed Fork, Relation size functions, reltuples and relpages, Default roles, pg_class, Index-only scan, pageinspect, Catalog, Tablespace, TID, TOAST, Autovacuum, Recursive CTE, Relfilenumber and Buffer manager without change. The term count is now 344; the glossary's own navigation blurbs in `wiki/index.md` and all five version landing pages were updated from 343. Verification fields remain `not yet`.
- Contradiction check: compared every statement, table row, map branch, worked example, script comment and last-run record on the new page with each other, with the pinned source, with the linked glossary entries, with its own index entries and coverage note, and with the v12 zeroed-file, amcheck and corruption-log pages and the v12 hits for `mdnblocks`, `RELSEG_SIZE`, `relpages`, `_fsm`, `_vm` and `pg_read_binary_file`. Found and fixed three: the lead said one case "left only the index witness" while the results table shows an ambiguous layout finding as well; the lead said "every relation with storage" while the SQL skips temporary tables; and six glossary navigation blurbs gave 343 terms after the new entry. None were reported without a change, and none were withdrawn. The default-plan versus sequential `pg_class` count was investigated and explained as an index-only scan (plan recorded), not filed as a contradiction. Earlier pages are consistent with this one; none was edited.
- Bookkeeping: added the page to the Storage and Vacuum lists of `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md`, and a dated coverage note to `wiki/versions.md`. ## [2026-10-21] review and fix v12 | DROP SCHEMA after emptied relation files

- Request and prompt hygiene: the review request was `follow agents.md, for postgresql 12, review question: # DROP SCHEMA After Relation Files Were Emptied in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)`. Corrections: "follow agents.md" → "Follow AGENTS.md"; "for postgresql 12" → "For PostgreSQL 12"; "review question:" → "review the question page"; the leading `# ` is Markdown heading syntax, not part of the title. None changed the meaning. The page's `## Question` was not touched.
- Scope and precedence: the request stated no scope, so under MANDATORY Review Requests the full review, including the contradiction check, was reported before any edit, followed by one scope question. The user chose "Fix all findings", which authorized fixing all eight reported findings on [DROP SCHEMA After Relation Files Were Emptied in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)](v12/questions/storage-and-vacuum/drop-schema-zeroed-relation-files.md) plus its bookkeeping. The optional standby/replay paragraph was offered separately and not chosen, so it was not added. The page is source-only, so nothing was re-run. No rule conflicted with the request.
- Evidence: only the PostgreSQL 12.2 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. Beyond the original page's sources, the review read: lock acquisition in `get_object_address()` and `findDependentObjects()`; the lock manager's wait-queue check and the lmgr README rules; foreign-key constraint dependencies, action-trigger creation and `RemoveTriggerById()`; partition-drop locks; `RelationInitPhysicalAddr()`; the `standard_ProcessUtility()` → `EventTriggerSupportsObjectType()` dispatch; index use in `systable_beginscan()`; buffer-miss reads; the `mdread()` short-read and `_mdfd_getseg()` segment-gap errors; and the `zero_damaged_pages` and `ignore_system_indexes` definitions.
- Fixes, numbered as reported:
  - F1, a contradiction with the source: the decision map placed all deletion locks before the `pg_depend` walk. The schema is actually locked inside `get_object_address()`, before the ownership check, and each dependent gets its deletion lock during the walk. `reportDependentObjects()` raises the `RESTRICT` error only afterwards ([objectaddress.c#lock-after-lookup](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/objectaddress.c#L1076-L1088), [dependency.c#lock-before-recursing](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/dependency.c#L883-L901), [dependency.c#report-after-walk](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/dependency.c#L401-L429)). The map, its keys, the value table, the lead, step 2, a new step 4 with its following paragraph, two "Cases" rows, the rollback lifecycle row, the causal summary and the Evidence Map now say that a plain `RESTRICT` drop can wait for those locks before refusing. They also explain how later requests queue, including the insert-ahead exception.
  - F2: the deletion-time lock list now includes foreign-key referenced tables. They are locked through action-trigger removal, can be outside the schema, and are not dropped.
  - F3: the emptied-catalog row now traces index-based `pg_depend` reads and the short-read and segment-gap errors. It gives default, cause and mechanism for `ignore_system_indexes` and `zero_damaged_pages`.
  - F4: the `rd_node` claim now cites `RelationInitPhysicalAddr()`.
  - F5: the dispatch step now cites the hop to `ProcessUtilitySlow()`.
  - F6: the `cat /dev/null >` truncation is now a stated assumption, filed under Open Questions.
  - F7: added first-use glossary links: Relation, Tablespace, Relfilenumber (as relfilenode), Relation mapper, Grammar, Extension, Declarative partitioning, Foreign key trigger, Invalidation message, Crash recovery, Dirty buffer and Lock queue.
  - F8: the storage list now includes materialized views and TOAST tables, and the storage-less kinds; the segment loop is described per fork; "installs links" became "symlinks"; the `sync.c#checkpoint-cycle` labels were corrected; and the direct event-trigger rejection test is cited.
- Glossary: reviewed the 16 entries the page already linked, the 12 newly linked ones and Truncation. All hold for PostgreSQL 12 as the page uses them, so no entry changed and the glossary keeps 346 terms. The developer settings `zero_damaged_pages` and `ignore_system_indexes` have no entries; the page explains each inline with its default, cause and mechanism, so no entry was added.
- Contradiction check: compared every statement, map branch, table row, example, summary, Evidence Map row and Open Question of the assembled page with the pin, with the linked glossary entries, with its two index entries, and with the v12 manifest row and coverage notes. Also compared other v12 pages that mention `mdunlink`, `RelationDropStorage`, `DROP SCHEMA`, `AcquireDeletionLock`, `zero_damaged_pages`, `ignore_system_indexes`, `EventTriggerSupportsObjectType` and the lock queue. Results:
  - One contradiction in the reviewed page (F1) was fixed, along with every copy of it.
  - During the fix, a draft sentence said every later request queues behind the waiting drop. That contradicted the insert-ahead exception stated by the v12 lmgr README and the Lock queue glossary entry ([lmgr/README#lock-acquisition-rules](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/README#L362-L381)), and it was corrected in all four places before filing.
  - No contradiction was found in other documents, and none was withdrawn. These agree with the page: the VACUUM FULL page's "until ENOENT" loop, the last-segment page's "until the next checkpoint", the zeroed-relation-file, corruption-log and health-checklist statements on `zero_damaged_pages`, and the navigation guide's dispatch description.
- Bookkeeping: this page's summaries in `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md` now keep the lock-before-refusal, outside-schema lock and catalog-error conditions. A dated coverage note was added to `wiki/versions.md`; the v12 version row does not mention the page and was left alone. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages, and none was created or changed.
- Verification: `verified: false` and `verified_by_agent: not yet` are unchanged; the title keeps `(unverified)`.

None changed the meaning. The page's own `## Question` is unchanged.

The user chose “Fix and measure”: fix every finding on the page and the glossary citation, update bookkeeping, and add a published measurement of the emptied-user-file behavior. No rule conflicted with the request. The measurement script uses only POSIX `ps` in its own stop check; the `pgrep -f -- <datadir>` confirmation that MANDATORY Environment Isolation requires was run by hand after teardown, so the POSIX-only rule for scripts and the teardown rule did not collide. - Evidence: only PostgreSQL 12.2 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. Re-read all 148 cited ranges of the reviewed page, then for the fixes: `CreateConstraintEntry()` dependencies and `RemoveConstraintById()`; trigger-function dependencies and `RemoveTriggerById()`; default and policy dependencies with `RemoveAttrDefaultById()` and `RemovePolicyById()`; `CheckTableForSerializableConflictIn()`, `SerializationNeededForWrite()`, `PredicateLockingNeededForRelation()`, `FlagRWConflict()` and the writer-failure branch; `get_object_namespace()`, `check_object_ownership()`, `pg_namespace_ownercheck()`, `has_privs_of_role()`, `roles_has_privs_of()`, `roles_is_member_of()` and the not-owner error; `_mdfd_getseg()`'s start segment; `guc.h`'s `PGC_SUSET` contract and the invalid-header zeroing in `bufmgr.c`; the namespace regression test and its expected output; and, for the measurement, the GUC entries it changes, the lock-timeout timer and error site, `errcodes.txt`, and the default `RELSEG_SIZE`.
- Review findings, each fixed in place:
  - F1, medium: the CASCADE lock list omitted the referencing side of a cross-schema foreign key. A foreign key has a normal dependency on the referenced columns, so CASCADE drops it, `RemoveConstraintById()` and `RemoveTriggerById()` take `AccessExclusiveLock` on the referencing table, and that table survives without its foreign key. The list also omitted outside triggers, defaults and policies that use schema functions. The lead, decision map, deletion section, Cases row, Evidence Map, causal summary and both index entries now say this, with the list marked as examples.
  - F2, contradiction: the lock table's “Insertion ahead of the drop is not mandatory” could be read as allowing a queue position behind the drop. `AccessExclusiveLock` conflicts with every mode, so `ProcSleep()` grants at once, reports an early deadlock, or queues the session at or ahead of the drop ([proc.c#existing-holder-queue-position](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/proc.c#L1100-L1204), [lock.c#LockConflicts](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/lock.c#L99-L103)). The row now says it never queues behind the drop.
  - F3, contradiction: the `_mdfd_getseg()` row said the function rejects a short earlier segment, naming only an open target as an exception. The size loop starts at the last open segment ([md.c#segment-already-open](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/smgr/md.c#L1112-L1141)), so an already-open earlier segment is trusted as full; the row is now qualified.
  - F4, contradiction: “not the owner -> error” omitted inheriting members of the owning role, which `has_privs_of_role()` passes ([aclchk.c#pg_namespace_ownercheck](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/aclchk.c#L4927-L4951), [acl.c#has_privs_of_role](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/utils/adt/acl.c#L4907-L4922)). The value table, map, key A and step 2 now give the exact check and the `42501` error.
  - F5: added a Cases row for a `SERIALIZABLE` dropping transaction, with default, cause and mechanism; it is source-derived and not measured.
  - F6: the zero-fill row now names `zero_damaged_pages`'s superuser-only context, and the silent-fetch paragraph scopes “only that error” to the short-read path, noting the invalid-header zeroing elsewhere.
  - F7: the test paragraph no longer calls the namespace test's post-drop count a catalog verification; that count cannot fail once the schema row is gone.
  - F8: added first-use glossary links to Heap, Page, Block, Deadlock, Lock mode, Role membership, and statement_timeout and lock_timeout.
- Measurement: a new `## Measurement Script` section publishes one Bash/SQL script. It builds the pin out of tree with `RELSEG_SIZE` patched to 128 blocks (1 MB) and runs a socket-only cluster on port 55444 with `autovacuum = off` and `checkpoint_timeout = 1h`. It empties all 29 files of schema `s`'s 14 relations with storage while stopped, then runs RESTRICT, a lock-contention leg, an ownership leg, a rolled-back CASCADE, a committed CASCADE with `CHECKPOINT`, and a removed-segment leg. Results: `2BP01` with 11 dependents; the waiting drop held the schema lock and `AccessExclusiveLock` on `s.t_id_seq` and waited on `s.t`; a new session timed out with `55P03`, while the lock-holding session re-acquired its mode and got `ROW EXCLUSIVE`; an inheriting member dropped `s_own` and a non-member got `42501`; CASCADE held `AccessExclusiveLock` on the three surviving `other` tables, and after commit only the 14 zero-length first main files remained, which one `CHECKPOINT` removed; `other.child_ref` lost its foreign key and `other.audit` its trigger; with `.1` removed, `.2` and `.3` survived as orphans with no warning. Every non-default setting is explained on the page. No measured result disagreed with the source reading.
- Runs: development runs found two script defects before filing: an undercounted RESTRICT pass message, and a stop-stage process check that matched the calling shell's command line. The final published text was extracted from the page with the published command and run twice from an empty sandbox (45 and 46 s), plus re-runs with the build present (2 to 3 s): 15 of 15 assertions each time, Darwin 27.0.0 arm64, block size 8192, maximum alignment 8, checksums off.
- Glossary: the Role membership PostgreSQL 12 note cited `roles_is_member_of()`, which ignores `rolinherit`, for inheritance by `rolinherit`. It now cites `has_privs_of_role()` and `roles_has_privs_of()`, which applies `rolinherit`, keeps `roles_is_member_of()` for plain membership tests, and links `pg_namespace_ownercheck()`. The PostgreSQL 12 source list lacked `aclchk.c`, newly cited, and `proc.c`, which the Lock queue note has cited since this morning's fix even though that entry said the inventory already covered its lock citations. Both were added, and the count now reads 389 files, matching the distinct files cited. No term was added; there are still 348 terms. Human verification fields and `verified_by_agent: not yet` are preserved.
- Contradiction check: compared every statement, table row, map branch, key, measured result, script comment, last-run record, Context Reviewed, Evidence Map and Open Question of the assembled page with the pin, with the 37 linked glossary entries and their PostgreSQL 12 notes, with both index entries and the v12 manifest row, and with v12 pages stating the same symbols: the health checklist, failure scenarios, zeroed-file, last-segment, corruption-log and comment-stored pages. Results:
  - On the reviewed page: 3 contradictions found (F2 to F4), all fixed with every copy.
  - Before filing, the assembled-page check also corrected draft statements: a mislabelled “second and third foreign-key cases”, a “no read” inference narrowed to main-fork pages, an index entry that had dropped the RESTRICT condition, and two statements still calling measured behavior source-derived.
  - Outside the page: 2 glossary defects, the Role membership citation and the missing `proc.c` and `aclchk.c` source entries, were fixed in place.
  - Reported without a change: the 2 previously recorded first-short-segment conflicts on the zeroed-file and last-segment pages are still present and still recorded under Open Questions.
  - Withdrawn: 3 candidates. The docs' “contains any objects” versus the normal-dependency rule has no differing ordinary-object case. “Locks come from two places” refers to relation locks. The comment-stored page's “requires ownership” is consistent with role-based ownership.
- Bookkeeping: updated this page's entries in `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md` and added a dated coverage note to `wiki/versions.md`; the v12 version row does not mention the page and was left alone. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages, and none was created or changed. `verified: false`, `verified_by_agent: not yet` and the `(unverified)` title are unchanged.
- Validation: 177 distinct citation ranges into 56 PostgreSQL 12 files resolve within their files; all 25 Contents entries match the headings in order; every in-page anchor and all 37 glossary anchors resolve; the embedded script is byte-identical to the script that ran, and `bash -n` passes; `git diff --check` passes. Pre-log lint reports the existing 61 errors and 2 warnings, all from v19 citations, the v19 pin and raw-checkout untracked files, with none in a changed file. The final lint run follows this entry.
- Isolation and teardown: the only service was the script's own cluster in `.wiki-runtime/tmp/ds12/`, on a private socket and port 55444. Twice, when `stop` ran in a later tool call, it found the cluster already stopped cleanly with no `postmaster.pid`; the cause was not identified. Once, before the fix, the stop check matched the calling shell and halted the deletion; the server was already stopped and a second `stop clean` finished. Final state, confirmed by hand: no `postmaster.pid`, no `pgrep -f` match for the data directory or the install, no listener on port 55444, and `.wiki-runtime/tmp/ds12/` deleted. The review-phase helpers in `.wiki-runtime/tmp/dsr3/` were deleted before the scope question; the extracted `.wiki-runtime/tmp/ds12-run.sh`, the run logs and the validation helpers in `.wiki-runtime/tmp/dsv3/` are removed after final validation. The pre-existing `.wiki-runtime/tmp/cmt17/` and `ptrev/`, the untracked `.pi/` and the raw checkouts' `.DS_Store` files were left alone. No commit or push was requested.

Other pages and rules documents stay outside this fix scope.
- Evidence: only the PostgreSQL 12.2 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. Beyond the earlier sources, the review read how `findDependentObjects()` locks each dependent during its `pg_depend` scan and recurses only after it ([dependency.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/dependency.c#L861-L901), [dependency.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/catalog/dependency.c#L960-L988)); `StartTransaction()`'s copy of the default isolation level ([xact.c:1953](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/access/transam/xact.c#L1953)) and `check_XactIsoLevel()` ([variable.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/commands/variable.c#L525-L554)); the `BEGIN` transaction-mode grammar and option handling; the `role` setting, `check_role()` and `is_member_of_role()`; and `deleteOneObject()`. A search of `dependency.c`, `heap.c`, `index.c`, `pg_constraint.c`, `rewriteRemove.c` and the removal routines in `trigger.c`, `policy.c`, `schemacmds.c` and `functioncmds.c` found no ownership or privilege check on dependents; `RemoveObjects()` checks only the named objects ([dropcmds.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/commands/dropcmds.c#L104-L109)).
- Fixes, numbered as reported:
  - 1 (medium, contradiction): the lead said that while the drop waits, new requests on “those tables” (all emptied tables) queue behind it, while the lock-queue section, Cases row and Evidence Map limit this to the awaited object. The walk requests its locks one at a time, so tables it has not reached carry no lock and stay lockable. The lead, step 3, the decision map and its key B, a new paragraph after the lock-queue table, the Cases row, the Evidence Map and the measured-results row now say this. A new measured probe backs it.
  - 2 (contradiction): `transaction_isolation` was said to be settable at session scope. `StartTransaction()` overwrites it at each transaction start and `check_XactIsoLevel()` refuses a change after the first snapshot, matching the glossary's Isolation level note. The page now separates the two settings, names `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL SERIALIZABLE` and adds an Evidence Map row.
  - 3 (contradiction): a script comment asserted that the shell's `>` uses `O_TRUNC`, which the page's Open Questions treats as an unestablished assumption. The comment now defers to the `empty` stage's 0-byte assertion.
  - 4: the Usage table's “the deletion section” pointer, stale since the sixth review moved the cross-schema measured note, now lists the lead, the lock-queue table and paragraph, Cross-schema dependencies and locks, Commit removes storage by filename and Cases.
  - 5: the Evidence Map's predicate-lock transfer row now keeps the eligibility condition (OID not below `FirstBootstrapObjectId`, not temporary).
  - 6: the Cases row says outside relations depend on objects in the schema, not on the schema.
  - 7: both index entries now limit the removed outside objects to those that depend on schema objects.
  - 8: `assert_sql_target`'s `SHOW` now carries the `wiki_ds12_target` tag.
  - 9: removed the dead `$BUILD/relseg_size.txt` alternative from the `RELSEG_SIZE` check.
  - 10: a Non-default settings row names the `role` setting set by `SET LOCAL ROLE`: `PGC_USERSET`, transaction scope, default `none`, and the `check_role()` membership test.
  - 11: the fixture list names its two functions, `s.f()` and `s.trg_fn()`.
  - 12: first-use links to [Snapshot](glossary.md#snapshot), [Tuple](glossary.md#tuple) and [Postmaster](glossary.md#postmaster).
  - 13: step 2, the cross-schema section and a new Evidence Map row say that ownership is checked only for the named schemas, so `CASCADE` also removes other roles' dependent objects outside the schema unless added code rejects it. This is source-derived; the Measured results gap list and Open Questions now include it.
- Measurement: the script gained one probe, which picks a table of `s` that the waiting drop holds no lock on and locks it under a 1 s `lock_timeout`. Extracted with the published command and checked with `bash -n`, it ran from an empty sandbox (64.18 s) and again with the build present (2.10 s) on `Linux 6.18.54.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2 x86_64`: PostgreSQL 12.2, block size 8192, segment size 128 blocks, maximum alignment 8, checksums off, `shared_buffers` 16384 blocks, read-committed isolation. All 16 assertions passed both times. The probe chose `s.big` and got `ACCESS SHARE` at once, while `locks_d_held.txt` showed the drop holding the schema lock, `s.t_id_seq` and its `pg_depend` locks and waiting for `s.t`, with no lock on `s.big`. The other 15 results matched the page: `2BP01` with 11 dependents, 29 and 7 emptied files, `55P03`, the immediate `ROW EXCLUSIVE` grant, `42501`, 11 relations after rollback, 14 deferred first main files, and the `.2`/`.3` orphans. The exclusion-constraint, serialization and cross-role statements remain source-derived. The post-run edits changed no text inside the script fence.
- Glossary: reviewed the 61 entries the page already linked and the newly linked Postmaster, Snapshot and Tuple; all carry PostgreSQL 12 scope and agree with the page, and the Isolation level PostgreSQL 12 note supports fix 2. No glossary edit was made; the glossary keeps 350 terms, and the page now links 64 distinct entries. Verification fields are unchanged.

Results:

  - The assembled-page check corrected 5 draft defects before filing: a universal “no removal routine checks ownership” claim narrowed to the core routines traced, with the hook and event-trigger exception; “system or temporary index” replaced by the exact `FirstBootstrapObjectId` condition; an uncited description of the `role` default; and two citation ranges (`guc.c` 3825–3835 to 3824–3834, `deleteOneObject()` 1327 to 1331).
  - Reported without a change, outside scope: the [failure-scenarios Open Questions note](v12/questions/storage-and-vacuum/schema-empty-relation-files-failure-scenarios.md#open-questions) at line 277 still says the zeroed-file and last-segment pages omit open-segment state, which their explanations now include ([md.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/smgr/md.c#L715-L737)); and [README.md](../README.md):47 still describes an ask-first typo rule that AGENTS.md replaced.
  - Withdrawn 3: the storage-less kinds list omitting composite types (an example list), the pre-commit “no” branch not mentioning that it may doom another pivot (an omission, not a false claim), and the `report` stage note naming fewer files than the Usage table (a subset).
- Bookkeeping: updated this page's entries in `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md` and added a dated Coverage Note to `wiki/versions.md`; the v12 version row does not mention the page and was left alone. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages for these mechanisms, and none was created or changed. `verified: false`, `verified_by_agent: not yet` and the `(unverified)` title are unchanged.

  - R2, contradiction: line 220 said `default_transaction_isolation` "can be set for a session or a transaction". A transaction-scoped value ends with that transaction, before any later one copies it ([xact.c:1953](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/access/transam/xact.c#L1953)). The page now names session-level, configuration-file and connection-start settings. This agrees with the glossary's Isolation level note.

  - R4, contradiction: the lead said new sessions can lock unreached tables "at once", while the body says "unless something else conflicts". The lead now says they can lock them without waiting for the drop.

New evidence for the fixes: `ProcSleep()` arms one deadlock timer per wait and runs `CheckDeadLock()` once when it fires ([proc.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/proc.c#L1248-L1297)); `DeadLockCheck()` reorders a queue on a soft deadlock and wakes its waiters ([deadlock.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/deadlock.c#L217-L285)); `deadlock_timeout` defaults to 1000 ms with `superuser` context ([guc.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/utils/misc/guc.c#L2062-L2072)); the shared lock table's sizing ([lock.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/lock.c#L56-L57), [postinit.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/utils/init/postinit.c#L532-L534)), its overflow error ([lock.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/lock.c#L956-L968)) and the fast-path rule ([lock.c](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/lock.c#L199-L204)); and the documentation on who may drop a schema ([drop_schema.sgml](../raw/postgres-12/doc/src/sgml/ref/drop_schema.sgml#L35-L39), [ddl.sgml](../raw/postgres-12/doc/src/sgml/ddl.sgml#L1580-L1582), [user-manag.sgml](../raw/postgres-12/doc/src/sgml/user-manag.sgml#L320-L329)).
- Fixes, numbered as reported:
  - 1 (medium, contradiction): the page said session A's 2 s `lock_timeout` showed that its requests did not queue behind the drop. A request queued behind the drop forms a soft deadlock with it, which A's own deadlock check resolves after the 1 s `deadlock_timeout` by reordering the queue, inside 2 s; the cited lmgr README says the same ([lmgr/README](../raw/postgres-12/src/backend/storage/lmgr/README#L370-L377)). Session A now uses a 500 ms bound, and the `locks` stage checks that `deadlock_timeout` is `1s`. Lock-queue rows 2 and 3, a new paragraph after the lock table, the `locks` measured row, the Stages row, the Non-default `lock_timeout` row, the Evidence Map row, the script comments and the pass message now say this.

  - 4: the causal summary now says outside objects that depend on objects in the schema.
  - 5: the lead and the Cases lock row now list non-`SELECT` rewrite rules, citing `RemoveRewriteRuleById()`'s table lock.
  - 6: lock-queue row 1 and the `locks` measured row name the baseline session and the second session separately.
  - 7: step 4 says the single-schema message applies when exactly one named schema was found; names that `IF EXISTS` skipped are not counted.
  - 8: first-use links to Subtransaction, HOT and Two-phase commit.
  - 9: the script header now names `ds12-run.sh`.

The other 15 assertions matched the twelfth review's run. The embedded script is byte-identical to the one that ran. Lock-table exhaustion and the serialization, exclusion-constraint and cross-role statements remain source-derived.
- Glossary: the Heavyweight lock entry's PostgreSQL 12 note gained the shared lock table's sizing from `max_locks_per_transaction` × (`MaxBackends` + `max_prepared_transactions`) in `InitLocks()`, the `out of shared memory` failure, the fast-path rule, and the setting's `postmaster` context and default of 64.   - R2, contradiction: the Non-default row said a queued request of A's "could be granted only by A's own deadlock check"; the drop's own check, or the drop ending, could also release it. The statement is now limited to the probe, after the drop's check has run and before the drop ends. The Evidence Map's garbled "outlasted" sentence was rewritten.
  - R3, contradiction: the new lock-table Cases row and Evidence Map row said the walk keeps the lock on every dependent, while step 3 lists the branches in which `findDependentObjects()` gives a lock back. Both now say every object it found, which the twelfth review's analysis supports: a given-back object either leaves the walk or is locked again through its owner.
  - R4, contradiction: the Open Questions bullet called the privileges passage of `ddl.sgml` and the role-membership passage of `user-manag.sgml` chapters; both are sections ([ddl.sgml](../raw/postgres-12/doc/src/sgml/ddl.sgml#L1580-L1582), [user-manag.sgml](../raw/postgres-12/doc/src/sgml/user-manag.sgml#L320-L329)).

## [2026-10-22] file and measure v12 | On-disk B-tree index TID check against the heap, without amcheck

- Request and prompt hygiene: the request was `follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , question: propose a sql using contrib extenssion to check all tid on a index againt its table heap to check if its tables doesn't have any data loss, it needs to check on disk only to bypass the shared buffers.`, followed by a second message, `don't use amcheck`. Both were combined into the page's `## Question`: "In PostgreSQL 12, propose SQL that uses a contrib extension to check every TID in an index against its table's heap, to verify that the table has no data loss. The check must read only from disk, bypassing shared buffers. Do not use amcheck." Corrections: dropped the instruction to the agent ("follow agents.md"); capitalized In, PostgreSQL and SQL; removed the space before the comma and the "question:" label; "a sql" became "SQL"; "extenssion" became "extension"; "all tid on a index" became "every TID in an index"; "againt" became "against"; "its table heap" became "its table's heap"; "to check if its tables doesn't have any data loss" became "to verify that the table has no data loss"; "it needs to check on disk only to bypass the shared buffers" became "The check must read only from disk, bypassing shared buffers"; "don't use amcheck" became "Do not use amcheck." None changed the meaning.
- Scope and precedence: MANDATORY Answer And File. The follow-up instruction excluded amcheck (rule 1, the user's explicit instruction); no rule conflicted with it. The page is filed under `indexing`: the question is about B-tree index entries and most cited code is pageinspect's B-tree decoder and `nbtree`. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages, so none was linked, created or changed.
- Evidence: only the PostgreSQL 12.2 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`; 52 source files, 238 citation links. The answer combines core `pg_read_binary_file()`, which reads with stdio outside the buffer manager, with contrib pageinspect's `bytea` decoders. pageinspect's own readers (`get_raw_page()`, `bt_page_items(text, int)`, `bt_page_stats()`) use shared buffers. In 12, `bt_page_items(bytea)` reads item bytes without bounds checks and `heap_page_items()` copies `lp_len - t_hoff` bytes without checking `t_hoff`, so the script validates every page in SQL and PL/pgSQL first and calls `heap_page_items()` only in a one-page self-test on `pg_class` block 0. Correctness rests on README.HOT, pruning, VACUUM's index-before-heap order, the never-shrinking line-pointer array, `BufferSync()`, the lock conflict table, `LOCK TABLE`'s refusal of TOAST tables, autovacuum's separate TOAST pass, `PREPARE`'s parse-time lock, and the base backup torn-block retry. Planning a query was rejected as a lock method because `_bt_getrootheight()` reads B-tree metapages through shared buffers.
- Measurement: the published Bash-and-SQL script builds the pin out of tree with `RELSEG_SIZE` 128 blocks, then runs one suite with data checksums off and one with them on, against fixtures damaged ten ways. On `Darwin 27.0.0 arm64` it ran from an empty sandbox (136 s) and again with the build present (48 s); every assertion passed both times with identical results apart from timings. Both suites: `PASS` for `t_ok` (38,854 TIDs; 325 redirect roots, 101 dead, 1,634 heap-only and 2,720 unused line pointers) and `t_big` (2,000,000 TIDs over 34,483 heap blocks and 12,093 index blocks, 10,340 ms checksums off, 10,475 ms on); `t_cached`, zeroed while its clean page stayed in shared buffers, `FAIL` with 29 `heap_page_all_zero` in each index, while `count(pad)` still returned 4,000 rows; after a restart `count(pad)` returned 3,971 and the index lookup no row, with no error; `t_trunc` 288 `heap_block_missing` per index; `t_midseg` two layout findings and 6,432 `heap_block_missing`; `t_stale_heap` 2 `offset_beyond_line_pointers` per index; `t_stale_index` 400 `line_pointer_unused`; `t_hot` 1 `points_to_heap_only_tuple`; `t_toast` 4 `heap_page_all_zero` in the TOAST index and a "missing chunk number 0" read error. With checksums off, `t_lpcorrupt` gave 1 `line_pointer_corrupt` per index, `t_badidx` a confirmed `bad_header` and `bad_items` page, and `t_flip` passed undetected; with checksums on, all three failed with confirmed `bad_checksum` pages. After a cold restart the check left 0 shared buffers of `t_ok`'s relations, against 901 after a plain `count(*)`. The `index_name` and `include_toast=off` options and four refusals (unlogged table, non-superuser, no pageinspect, foreign index; exit status 3) were also checked. The SQL and the script embedded in the page are byte-identical to the ones that ran. A first draft of the helpers was about 50 times slower, because strict SQL functions with `CASE` are not inlined; it was replaced before the measured runs, and the page does not report that development timing.
- Glossary: added [Torn page](glossary.md#torn-page), checked on PostgreSQL 12 only, with its Contents line, a dated Scope note, and two new PostgreSQL 12 source references (`basebackup.c`, `checksum_impl.h`; 395 files). The term count is 351 in the Scope note and in the glossary descriptions on `wiki/index.md` and all five version landing pages. Reviewed the 33 entries the page links, among them pageinspect, TID, Line pointer, Data checksums, OS page cache, HOT, Pruning, Lock mode, Unlogged relation, Index-only scan, Visibility map, Metapage, All-zero page and amcheck; all exist with PostgreSQL 12 scope and agree with the page. Pivot tuple, checked on 17 only, was not linked; the page relies on the Leaf page entry's PostgreSQL 12 note for the high key. `verified` and `verified_by_agent` are unchanged.
- Contradiction check: compared the whole page (lead, value table, logic map and keys, the invariant and verdict table, lock and lifecycle tables, validation table, SQL comments, measured results, limits table, summary, script and its comments, Usage, Last run, Stages and Non-default tables, Context Reviewed, Evidence Map and Open Questions) with the pin, the 33 linked glossary entries, the page's own index entries, the v12 manifest row, and the v12 pages that state the same claims (the relation-tail check, the amcheck follow-up, the zeroed-file page, the corruption-log reference and the invalid-index page). Found and fixed 6 contradictions on the page before filing: the lead said two damage kinds needed checksums, but only one did; condition 1 dropped the "dirty when the checkpoint starts" condition; "every SQL-callable page reader" ignored `pg_prewarm`'s `read` mode; the not-ready-index sentence went beyond the source's wording; the autovacuum sentence ignored the cancellation of a blocking worker; and "damage done with `dd`" missed `cp` and a shell redirection. Also fixed 2 citations that ran past the end of `pageinspect--1.5--1.6.sql`.
