The review re-read about 130 of the page's 198 distinct citation targets (all planner, EXPLAIN, MVCC/HOT, RLS, lock, GUC, catalog and pageinspect claims) and found no wrong engine claim. The fixes added evidence from `ruleutils.c` (key parenthesization, bare `integer`/`numeric` constant printing, function-name qualification), `gram.y` (redundant-parenthesis handling), `guc.c` (`operator_precedence_warning` default), `analyze.c` and `parse_clause.c` (SQL92 `ORDER BY` targets), `parse_coerce.c` (same-type no-op), `equivclass.c`, `pathnodes.h` and `pathkeys.c` (constant pathkey redundancy), `utility.c` (`check_xact_readonly()`), `allpaths.c` (partial paths), `costsize.c` (`cost_sort()`), `postgres.c` (timeout errors), `procarray.c` (first snapshot sets `TransactionXmin`) and `installation.sgml` (Git-checkout build tools).
- Findings and fixes: (1) Medium: the generator wrapped `pg_get_indexdef(oid, n, false)` in parentheses only. The grammar drops redundant parentheses under the default `operator_precedence_warning = off`, and SQL92 `ORDER BY` rules read a bare integer as an output position and reject other bare constants, so a key on `(2)` or `(1.5)` failed and became a relation ERROR; the measured `(1)` case had passed only because position 1 is the inner query's constant. `hic_index_keys` now deparses each key once through a guarded `LATERAL` and emits a bare numeric-literal key as `CAST(<key> AS <its type>)`; step 2, the constant-key path explanation, the generation-policy paragraph, the test matrix and the review table say so. (2) Medium: `verified_by_agent` returned to `not yet`. (3) Reason codes in the inventory/eligibility prose now match the emitted codes (`unsupported_table_access_method`, `partitioned_parent_summary`, `view`/`foreign_table`/`sequence`, `index_metadata`, `outside_physical_heap_scope`, `partial_index`, `invalid_index`, `index_not_ready`, `index_not_live`, `nonphysical_index`), and the outside-schema partition is described by `hic_partition.outside_schema` and `outside_schema_descendants`. (4) The record table now describes the stored error fields and the derived `uncovered_error_attempts`, splits parent and schema summaries, and `hic_schema_result` gained eligible/checked/matching/untested index totals and `all_tested_indexes_match`; `error_template` gained detail and hint slots and step 7 names them. (5) The script's `pgrep` became a POSIX `ps` listing written to `processes.txt` and searched with `grep -F`. (6) Prerequisites now name GNU make, a C99 compiler, Bison, Flex and Perl. (7) First-use glossary links were added for prepared statement, hot standby, SPI, pg_class, pg_index, inheritance, partitioned index, TOAST, unlogged relation, planner, relcache, transient plan, transaction ID, bitmap scan, isolation level, xmin horizon, disable_cost, plan node instrumentation and EXPLAIN SETTINGS. (8) Mechanism citations were added for `max_parallel_workers_per_gather`, `enable_sort`, `search_path`, both timeouts and the read-only `CREATE` refusal. (9) `same_server` sets session timeouts, `createdb` became a tagged `CREATE DATABASE` with timeouts, the Purpose row names the claims it backs, and the value table gained a live/stored column and the `indcheckxmin`, `pg_index.xmin` and `TransactionXmin` rows.
- Measurement: the published script, extracted from the page and checked with `bash -n`, ran from an empty sandbox (prepare 1 s, build 99 s, init 0 s, verify 16 s) and again with the build retained (verify 15 s), on Linux 6.18.54.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2 x86_64, PostgreSQL 12.2 built with GCC 13.3.0, block size 8192, maximum alignment 8, UTF-8, C collation/ctype, checksums off. Both runs passed every assertion with identical per-case totals (positive 42, external 36, negative 29, choice 10, concurrent 9, verdict units 23, 33 plan-shape units). New results: the fixture probe raised SQLSTATE `42P10` ("ORDER BY position 2 is not in select list") for `((2))` and `42601` ("non-integer constant in ORDER BY") for `((1.5))`, while the `CAST` forms ran; the negative schema now has 8 physical relations, 4 NO_ELIGIBLE_INDEX and 4 INCONCLUSIVE, with `constant_literals` giving 4 rejected index attempts and no attempt error; the positive schema reports 13 eligible, checked and matching indexes with 0 mismatches; the external closure 14 of 14; the choice schema 2 eligible, 1 checked and matching, 1 untested. All previously filed numbers were reproduced. The embedded script is byte-identical to the one that ran.
- Glossary: four entries checked only on PostgreSQL 17 gained PostgreSQL 12 notes verified against the pin, and their checked-on lines now name 12: disable_cost (value, every penalized switch, full-join bypass, no `enable_memoize`, `enable_indexonlyscan` gate), EXPLAIN SETTINGS (12 loops over every GUC; `row_security`, `track_io_timing` and `autovacuum` are not flagged), Plan node instrumentation (no WAL usage in 12; per-loop division; parallel aggregation) and Transient plan (planner flag and `saved_xmin` invalidation). Structure: 15 `eval` statements spread through the session functions; 56 numeric field references such as `${HIC_R[15]}` into 17- and 19-column fact lines; `hic_reports` as one 107-line function with 68 locals that reused `eligible`, `reason`, `schemaname` and `relname` for relations and then indexes; `hic_attempt_once` with five nested `if` levels and return-code juggling; `hic_validate` reading D, N and O records into variables named for P records; unused locals in seven functions plus an unused `HIC_SETTINGS_HEX`; a test-only `hic_encode` in the runner whose comment said "numeric identity fragments"; and hard-to-read `HIC_GUARD` quoting with unexplained literals (16384, 11, 403, `x68656170`, `x6869635f636f756e74`). Invalid yes/no options and unexpected arguments also exited 64 without a message.
- Changes: session state now goes through `hic_session` and `hic_session_set` (indirect expansion and `printf -v`); `eval` remains only to open and close numbered descriptors (4 statements). `hic_read_relation`, `hic_read_index` and named key, run and plan-record fields replace every numeric index (0 remain). `hic_load` and `hic_save` read and write several evidence files at once. `hic_attempt_once` delegates to `hic_attempt_run`, a stage sequence returning 0, 1 or 2, and to `hic_attempt_error`. `hic_reports` is split into relation, candidate, attempt and parent functions (18 locals left). `hic_parse_error` is pure Bash. Corrections: capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, In and PostgreSQL; removed the spaces before the commas and the final period, the doubled space, the `question:` label and the `#` marker; "review question" became "review the question page"; "fix issues" became "fix the issues"; "don't fix ." became "but do not fix them"; split into sentences. The approval `implement plan` became "Implement the plan." (capitalization, article, final period). No correction changed the meaning. Both are recorded as a 2026-10-24 follow-up under the page's `## Question`, which is otherwise unchanged.
- Scope and rule precedence: the first request was report-only by the asker's explicit "do not fix" (MANDATORY Rule Precedence item 1, MANDATORY Review Requests), so the review was static and the plan was given in chat. Three decisions were asked in one call: the asker chose a new relation status for anomalies, SQL-only unresolved-TID counters plus documentation for heap-side loss, and one physical fault-injection leg. Two stated defaults were then accepted with `implement plan`: the unresolved-TID count never changes a status, and the physical leg also damages a second block's header. The new `PASS_WITH_ANOMALY` status extends the Question's six statuses at the asker's request.
- Findings (all verified against the PostgreSQL 12.2 pin): (FN1) heap-side data loss passes, because an index scan silently skips TIDs that reach no row (`heap_hot_search_buffer`, `index_getnext_slot`) and both counts drop equally; the first such scan also kills the entries (`kill_prior_tuple`, `_bt_killitems`). (FN2) a mismatching accepted index or an XX/58 error beside a match exited 0. (FN3) `zero_damaged_pages` zeroes a bad page into a valid buffer that a retry reuses unverified, after which the earlier error counted as covered; `ignore_checksum_failure` reports only a WARNING with SQLSTATE 01000, because `bufpage.c` omits `errcode()`, and the runner ignored class 01. (FN4) TRUNCATE, rewrite, REFRESH, drop and re-create, and index renames after the snapshot were undetected; `relfilenode` was collected but unused, and EXPLAIN prints current index names. (FN5) unchecked evidence reads could drop a relation from the denominator. Edge cases: `"?column?"` key capture by `ORDER BY` output-name matching, a dollar-quote tag matching a value's end, role-level `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`, `old_snapshot_threshold` (the index-path heap fetch never calls `TestForOldSnapshot`), partition subtree gaps absent from the summary, and self-cancelling misdirected TIDs (left as an Open Question).
- Development check, not a page result: the original runner, run by a one-off script against the same sandbox, gave PASS and exit 0 for the invalid-header table with `zero_damaged_pages=on` (attempt 1 XX001 "zeroing out page", attempt 2 accepted 242 rows), PASS for the zeroed block (heap 242), INCONCLUSIVE for the `"?column?"` table, and PASS for TRUNCATE (heap 0) and REFRESH (post-snapshot rows) committed after its snapshot. This confirmed FN1, FN3, FN4 and E1 empirically.
- Implementation: the runner adds `hic_server_guard`, `hic_identity`, `hic_tid_counters`, `hic_unresolved_tids` and `hic_matched`; records index `relfilenode`; resolves executed index names through `to_regclass()`; fails a count attempt that prints a class-01 report; never covers XX/58/01 errors; derives `PASS_WITH_ANOMALY`; builds the inner count query with an empty select list; tightens `hic_literal`; sets `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 0`; checks every evidence read in verdict and report code; and adds anomaly, storage-error, unresolved-TID, drift and incomplete-subtree fields to the reports and summary. The harness gains edge, idle, drift, unreadable-evidence and damage cases, guard and warning units, settings refusals and six evaluator units; `synth` writes every file the evaluator reads.
- Measurement: from an absent sandbox, `prepare build` (build 79 s) and `init verify` (167 s) passed all 220 assertions; a second `verify` with the build retained (170 s, 12:44 to 12:46 UTC) passed all 220; `cleanup` took 2 s. Platform: Darwin 27.0.0 arm64, Apple clang 21.0.0, GNU Bash 5.3.20, block size 8192, maximum alignment 8, UTF-8 with C locale, checksums off. The zeroed block held 58 of 300 rows of a 6-block heap: PASS with heap 242 and `unresolved_index_tids=58`. The 0xFF block gave two XX001 heap errors and relation ERROR. The deleted-row fixture gave 10 unresolved TIDs, then 0 on the next run. The published runner and harness blocks were byte-identical to the executed files.
- Glossary: added [Killed index tuple](glossary.md#killed-index-tuple), checked on PostgreSQL 12 only, with its Contents line and a dated Scope note; qualified the PostgreSQL 12 note of [Data checksums](glossary.md#data-checksums) for `zero_damaged_pages`, recovery and `ignore_checksum_failure`. The PostgreSQL 12 inventory gained `nbtutils.c` and `genam.c` (412 files). The term count is 356 in the Scope note and in the glossary descriptions on `wiki/index.md` and all five version landing pages. Reviewed All-zero page, Dead tuple, Index scan, Line pointer, TID, Table rewrite, TRUNCATE, Cumulative statistics, Pruning, Snapshot, REINDEX, Relfilenumber, indcheckxmin, xmin horizon and Subtransaction for PostgreSQL 12; no other change was needed. Human verification fields are unchanged.
- Contradiction check: compared the whole assembled page (Question scopes, lead, value table, logic map, inventory, DDL, settings and refused-settings tables, query shapes, capture steps, validation gates, records table, status order and schema formula, limits and lifecycle tables, heap-side loss walkthrough, bag diagnostic, transport, branch and Running tables, test matrix, measured coverage, runner and harness, Measurement Script tables and last-run record, Context Reviewed, Evidence Map, Open Questions) with the pin, the linked glossary entries, both own index entries and the v12 manifest. Found and fixed 6 on the page: the limits row said storage errors always give ERROR, while a match now gives `PASS_WITH_ANOMALY`; "A matching actual index can establish the requested existential PASS even when another attempt failed" was qualified to ordinary failures; the `old_snapshot_threshold` row implied an error always follows; three DDL statements read as enforced invalidation (now scoped to the detected identity drift); the records table's Errors row attributed detail/hint to `run-errors.csv`; and the test-matrix row "one match plus another failure" now says ordinary failure. Fixed 1 in the glossary: the Data checksums PostgreSQL 12 note omitted the `zero_damaged_pages`/recovery zero-fill branch of `mdread()`. Outside-document contradictions: 0. Withdrawn: 0. Pre-existing duplicate citation labels for one range are cosmetic and unchanged.
- Bookkeeping and validation: updated the page's two index summaries and added a dated Coverage Note; the v12 version row is unchanged. 546 page citation links into 142 PostgreSQL 12 files resolve in range, every file is in Source References (19 added), all 28 Contents entries match the headings, all internal anchors and 51 glossary anchors resolve. `verified: false`, `verified_by_agent: not yet` and the `(unverified)` title are unchanged. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages; none was created or edited.
- Isolation and teardown: the only service was the harness's own socket-only cluster under `.wiki-runtime/tmp/hic12/` (port label 55447), started by each `verify` (and stopped and restarted once inside it for the physical leg) and by the one-off development script, each of which stopped it on exit. No subagent, network, commit or push was used; the pre-existing untracked `.pi/` and `.wiki-runtime/tmp/` items `cmt17`, `hicr` and `ptrev` were not touched.

- Request and prompt hygiene: the original request was `follow agents.md, in postgresql 12, review question: # SQL Heap and Index Visible-Count Checker in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)`. - Runner and regression fixes: `hic_get()` now reads complete evidence lists before iteration in verdicts, scheduling and reports, so an unreadable list cannot act like an empty one. `hic_contains()` distinguishes a read failure from an absent set member, and `wc` failures propagate before arithmetic. The existing harness adds ten assertions: missing-file and directory substitutions for `relation-ids`, `partitions.raw`, `index-ids` and `attempt-ids` all return 2; restoring the files restores successful reporting; and `hic_finish()` propagates a missing partition-facts file as failure. Both use a compact LSN decision table and PostgreSQL 12 evidence; the question page links the existing WAL and LSN entries. The `track_counts` row now describes statistics-socket startup failure forcing that setting off and reaching the runner's refusal. ## [2026-10-25] file v12 | How amcheck checks B-tree indexes and their tables, what it finds, its limits, and how data checksums change them

- Request and prompt hygiene: the request was `follow agents.md, in postgresql 12,  question: explain how amcheck work for indexes and tables, explain also in details what issues it finds and what are its limitations and how having data checksums enabled or not reduces amcheck limitations.`, corrected to "Follow AGENTS.md. In PostgreSQL 12, explain how amcheck works for indexes and tables. Also explain in detail what issues it finds, what its limitations are, and how having data checksums enabled or not reduces amcheck's limitations." Corrections: capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, In and PostgreSQL; removed the doubled space and the `question:` label; "work" became "works"; "in details" became "in detail"; "what are its limitations" became "what its limitations are"; "amcheck limitations" became "amcheck's limitations"; split the run-on into two sentences with a serial list. No correction changed the meaning. The corrected text is the page's `## Question`.
- Premise: PostgreSQL 12's amcheck has no table-target function; the Answer says so and explains that a table is checked only through `heapallindexed`. No rule conflict arose; no subagent, network access, commit or push was used.
- Filed [How amcheck Checks B-Tree Indexes and Their Tables in PostgreSQL 12, What It Finds, Its Limits, and How Data Checksums Change Them (unverified)](v12/questions/indexing/amcheck-indexes-tables-and-data-checksums.md) under `indexing` (subsystem rule: the page is about B-tree index verification and most citations are `verify_nbtree.c`), at unchanged pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. Deep inquiry read `verify_nbtree.c` in full, the three SQL scripts, control file, Makefile, regression SQL and `amcheck.sgml`, plus the core boundary (`heapam_index_build_range_scan()`, `heapgetpage()`, `index_form_tuple()`, `_bt_checkpage()`, lock tables, `bloomfilter.c`) and the checksum path (`ReadBuffer_common()`, `FlushBuffer()`, `PageIsVerified()`, `PageSetChecksumCopy()`, `pg_checksum_page()`, `mdread()`, GUC definitions, `initdb -k`, `pg_checksums`, base backup verification). Findings beyond the earlier zeroed-segment page: `STRICT` overloads return null without checking when an argument is null; the table phase clears `ii_Unique`, so no uniqueness check exists; the missing-downlink check needs both the parent check and `heapallindexed`; TOAST errors surface as `XX000`; power-of-two rounding gives about 1.05 bytes per Bloom entry even with ample memory, and for a 100-million-row index `maintenance_work_mem` stops helping at 128 MB; the checksum mixes in the block number but no relation identity and is computed at write time; `pg_checksums` and base backups skip any page with `pd_upper == 0`, while the server rejects such a page unless it is all zero.
- Docs-versus-source discrepancies filed under Open Questions: `amcheck.sgml` says only superusers may use the functions, while the scripts revoke `EXECUTE` from `PUBLIC`, the C code skips relation permission checks and the regression test runs the checks as a granted ordinary role; the docs' "presence of all heap tuples" is broader than the snapshot, predicate and HOT rules in the code. Source wins in both.
- Measurement: none. The two Bloom sizing examples and the 1-in-65,535 checksum calculation are arithmetic from the pinned formulas, not numbers produced by running PostgreSQL, so no `## Measurement Script` section applies. No service was started, so nothing needed teardown; the helper scripts under `.wiki-runtime/tmp/amck/` are deleted before completion.
- Glossary: added [Bloom filter](glossary.md#bloom-filter), checked on PostgreSQL 12 only, with its Contents line; added a PostgreSQL 12 note to [Base backup](glossary.md#base-backup), now checked on 12, 17 and 19; extended the PostgreSQL 12 note of [Data checksums](glossary.md#data-checksums) with block-number mixing, write-time computation and the unverified buffer-hit path; added a dated Scope bullet; the PostgreSQL 12 Source References inventory gained four files (417). The term count is 357 in the Scope note and in the glossary descriptions on `wiki/index.md` and all five version landing pages. Reviewed for the page without change: amcheck, B-tree, Heap, All-zero page, Torn page, Killed index tuple, Hint bits, Full-page image, Checkpoint, WAL, Control file, Lock mode, Hot standby, Metapage, Fast root, Sibling link, Leaf page, Page split, B-tree page deletion, Ring buffer, Line pointer, TID, HOT, TOAST, Partial index, MVCC, Snapshot, Operator class, Collation, Fork, Visibility map, Free space map, shared_buffers and Contrib. Pivot tuple and Suffix truncation are checked on 17 only, so the page does not link them. Human verification fields are unchanged.
- Contradiction check: compared every statement on the assembled page (lead, value table, logic map and key, overload, target-check, walk, per-page, parent-check, heap-phase, findings, limitations, Bloom calculations, checksum diagram and properties, off/on comparison, tool table, special-case and lifecycle tables, SQL sketch, build/test notes, causal summary, Context Reviewed, Evidence Map, Open Questions) with the pinned sources, the linked glossary entries, the page's own index entries and the v12 manifest row, and with same-version claims in the zeroed-segment amcheck page, the corruption-log page, the post-drop health page and the B-tree bloat page's contrib-boundary sentence. Found and fixed 3 on the page while drafting: a forks row said only the two main forks are read although indexed TOAST values are fetched; a statement that all corruption errors use `XX001`/`XX002` although TOAST errors default to `XX000`; and "bad page headers" in the lead and findings table, which amcheck does not itself check beyond zero pages and the special area. Contradictions with other documents: 0. Withdrawn: 0.
- Validation: all 380 page citations into 46 PostgreSQL 12 files resolve within range; all 25 Contents entries match the headings; all internal anchors and 35 glossary anchors resolve; the glossary's PostgreSQL 12 citations all resolve. `git diff --check` passes. PostgreSQL 12 has no common-concept pages; none was created or edited. Proposed for a separate task: v12 common-concept pages for B-tree integrity checking and for data checksums and page verification.

On Linux 6.18.54.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2 x86_64 with GCC 15.2.0, C99 mode, 8192-byte blocks, alignment 8, UTF-8/C locale, session UTC, shared buffers 128 MB and maintenance memory 64 MB, all 37 checks passed; build took 34 seconds and the successful verify stage 4 seconds. The free-space fault passed without checksums and failed with them. With checksums on, `track_counts = off` suppressed the counter but not the error; the enabled leg incremented it to 1. A single-mismatch backup failed without increasing that 1. An 8,193-byte relation file with a damaged complete page was copied by a successful backup with a verification warning. That result is not a restore test.

In PostgreSQL 12, for the question page 'How amcheck Checks B-Tree Indexes and Their Tables in PostgreSQL 12, What It Finds, Its Limits, and How Data Checksums Change Them (unverified)', add a follow-up question." The filed follow-up text is "Using amcheck, are index entries whose heap rows vanished not reported? Why not? Is there a way to perform this check?" Corrections: capitalized Follow, AGENTS.md, In, PostgreSQL and each sentence; dropped the `#` heading marker and the space before the comma; hyphenated "follow-up"; added the comma after "Using amcheck"; turned the first statement-with-question-mark into a question. The page's original Question text is unchanged.
- Deliverable: added `### Follow-up: why vanished heap rows go unreported` and `### Follow-up: checking for dangling index entries` to [the amcheck page](v12/questions/indexing/amcheck-indexes-tables-and-data-checksums.md), with Contents, Short-answer and limits-table links, results rows, settings rows, Context Reviewed, Evidence Map, Open Questions and Source References updates. Why: the `heapallindexed` design is a one-way subset test; the Bloom filter API has no enumeration or removal; and index entries naming dead tuples, `LP_DEAD` and `LP_REDIRECT` stubs, or `INDEX_CLEANUP FALSE` leftovers are legitimate. How: a superuser `pageinspect` statement under `LOCK TABLE ... IN SHARE MODE` that reports leaf entries naming a block past the end, a missing or unused line pointer, or a heap-only tuple. The "VACUUM could race a reverse probe under `AccessShareLock`" point is labelled an inference from the lock table, not a stated reason.

Ran `build verify interrupt cleanup` on 2026-10-26, Darwin 27.0.0 arm64, Apple clang 21.0.0, PostgreSQL 12.2 pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`: 67 of 67 checks passed (build 38 s, verify 6 s, interrupt 2 s). ## [2026-10-28] fourth review and fix v12 | Consequences of enabling data checksums

- Prompt hygiene: original "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , review question: # Consequences of Enabling Data Checksums in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)". Corrections: "agents.md" -> "AGENTS.md", "postgresql" -> "PostgreSQL", removed the space before the comma. The meaning is unchanged. The filed `## Question` is not modified.
- Scope: the request named no scope, so the static review was reported before any edit (MANDATORY Review Requests). The user then chose "Fix and re-measure". The page is source-only and has no `## Measurement Script`, so there was nothing to re-run, and no measurement was added.
- Fixes on [the page](v12/questions/storage-and-vacuum/enabling-data-checksums-consequences.md), at unchanged pin `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`:
  1. Replay row: a rejected redo read stops the server rather than only stalling replay. `XLogReadBufferForRedo()` uses `RBM_NORMAL` (`xlogutils.c:289-293`), and `StartupProcessMain()` has no `sigsetjmp` handler (`startup.c:173-213`), so `ERROR` becomes `FATAL` (`elog.c:260-269`). In crash recovery the postmaster is still in `PM_STARTUP`, so it logs "aborting startup due to startup process failure" and exits (`postmaster.c:2925-2936`). In archive recovery `PMSIGNAL_RECOVERY_STARTED` has moved it out of `PM_STARTUP` (`xlog.c:6999-7005`, `postmaster.c:5104-5110`), so `HandleChildCrash()` runs and the postmaster exits without reinitializing (`postmaster.c:2947-2955`, `3900-3909`). The error-context paragraph now names replay as the branch that does stop the server.
  2. `zero_damaged_pages` row: a later insert keeps the stale visibility-map bit. `PageInit()` sets `pd_flags = 0` (`bufpage.c:42-60`), and `heap_insert()` clears the map only under `PD_ALL_VISIBLE` (`heapam.c:1916-1922`). So index-only scans skip visibility checks for the new rows until a VACUUM that reads the page warns and clears the bit (`vacuumlazy.c:1318-1331`); VACUUM can skip such pages (`vacuumlazy.c:566-578`). Source-derived, not measured.
  3. `pg_resetwal` row: force is needed unless the state is exactly `DB_SHUTDOWNED` (`pg_resetwal.c:500-508`), so a standby shut down in recovery also needs it.
  4. File-name failure rule (error-table rows and backup note [A]): the test is `atoi()` of the text after the first dot giving 0 (`basebackup.c:1431-1440`, `pg_checksums.c:333-344`), not "a non-number".
  - Also updated the Evidence Map, Context Reviewed and Source References.
- Glossary: reviewed Data checksums (with its PostgreSQL 12 note), Visibility map, Hint bits, Full-page image, LSN, Base backup and Critical section. No change needed. No PostgreSQL 12 common-concept pages exist.
- Contradiction check:
  - Compared: the whole page, the pinned source, the linked glossary entries, both index entries, the v12 `versions.md` row and notes, and the sibling detection-coverage, mismatched-replication, corruption-log and disable-checksums pages, found by searching `wiki/v12`, `wiki/index.md` and the glossary for the replay, `pg_resetwal` force, visibility-map and segment-name wording.
  - Found 1 contradiction: line 167's clean-shutdown wording (which includes `DB_SHUTDOWNED_IN_RECOVERY`) against the `pg_resetwal` row's "force only when not shut down cleanly". The source (`pg_resetwal.c:500-508`) settles it. Counts: 1 fixed, 0 reported without change, 0 withdrawn. The other three findings were understatements or imprecisions against the source, and all were fixed. No copy of a changed claim exists on another page. Re-checking the changed statements found no new conflict. Dated records were not rewritten.

  1. Lock-table exhaustion (lead, the Cases row, the Evidence Map row): `NLOCKENTS()` is an estimate, not a fixed capacity. `InitLocks()` preallocates half of it (`lock.c:384-389`), `ShmemInitHash()` says the maximum is not a hard limit (`shmem.c:303-310`), and `LockShmemSize()` adds a 10% margin (`lock.c:3441-3460`). `53200` is raised when `SetupLockInTable()` cannot allocate a `LOCK` or `PROCLOCK` entry (`lock.c:1119-1180`, `956-968`).
  2. Open Questions: filed the docs-versus-source discrepancy. `config.sgml:8622-8630` and the `guc.c:2464-2468` help text size the table from `max_connections` and call the count a limit.
  3. `s2` fixture wording (Measured results, Last run, script comment): the `empty` stage empties all 8 files of `s2` and then removes `.1`, which leaves 7 at 0 bytes. The page had said `.1` was "removed instead of emptied".
  - Also updated Last run and Context Reviewed.
- Measurement: the script changed only in a comment. It was extracted with the page's own command and run from an empty sandbox (54.05 s). It was then run with the build present, once with the stages `fixture` to `report` (2.93 s) and once with the default stages (2.95 s). All 16 assertions passed each time, and every number matches the seventeenth review's run. No measured result disagrees with the source.
- Glossary: reviewed every term the page links. Fixed the PostgreSQL 12 note of "Heavyweight lock", which repeated the fixed-capacity wording, with v12 citations. `verified_by_agent: not yet` kept.
- Contradiction check: compared the whole page with itself, the pinned source and docs, the linked glossary entries, its `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md` entries, the v12 `versions.md` row, and the v12 pages that state lock-table sizing (`database-health-checklist.md`, `partitioned-index-parent-vs-child.md`) or the drop's file outcomes (`health-after-zeroed-schema-drop.md`, `schema-empty-relation-files-failure-scenarios.md`). Found 3 contradictions: the page against the source on lock-table capacity, the docs against the source (filed under Open Questions), and the page's `s2` prose against its script. Counts: 3 fixed in scope, plus the glossary copy of the first one; 0 reported without change; 0 withdrawn. The other pages give only the formula, which is correct.
- Bookkeeping: appended review notes to both index entries and added a coverage note to `versions.md`. Human `verified:` and `verified_by_agent: not yet` preserved.

  2. Contradiction: line 167 said "same-transaction `TRUNCATE`", while the branches and state tables said "current subtransaction".   3. Non-default `shared_buffers`: named both defaults, the 1,024-buffer boot value (`guc.c:2155-2163`) and `initdb`'s first 16,384-buffer (128 MB) trial (`initdb.c:959-962`, `1018-1045`, `1096-1102`); said which is compared, and why the script sets it.

Index TID witness: documented in prose and in the branches table that an index that lost a middle segment makes the query fail with `block number out of range`, because its page range comes from `pg_relation_size()` while `bt_page_stats()` checks `RelationGetNumberOfBlocks()` (`btreefuncs.c:52-54`, `:201`).

A first draft said "at least every `checkpoint_timeout`"; that was softened before filing, because a timed checkpoint can be skipped when no WAL was written.

Source-only: defines over-fetching as rows a node reads and does not pass up, maps each discard to its executor counter and EXPLAIN line, and lists twelve factors (row estimates, non-indexable conditions, non-boundary B-tree conditions, lossy rechecks and bitmaps, dead tuples and `relallvisible`, join strategy, CTE/subquery/function-scan fences, security barriers, LIMIT/EXISTS/cursor fractions, generic plans, unpruned partitions, cost settings), with a logic map, value table, settings table with defaults and apply scopes, lifecycle table, an illustrative (not measured) LIMIT calculation, extension boundaries and tests.

Added three v12 files to Source References (421 → 424). Semi join and anti join (checked on 17 and 19 only) is not linked from the page.

Found 4 in my draft before filing, all fixed: `relallvisible` described as set only by `VACUUM` (ANALYZE also writes it, analyze.c:594-603), the hash-join empty-outer shortcut missing its outer-join and right/full-join conditions, the CTE "second reference" fence missing the `NOT MATERIALIZED` exception, and a `constraint_exclusion` row that overstated its scope. ## [2026-10-30] review and fix v12 | Planner factors that produce plans that over-fetch tuples

- Prompt hygiene: review request "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , review question: # Planner Factors That Produce Plans That Over-Fetch Tuples in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)", then "fix all , commit and review again". No correction changes the meaning; the only change is dropping the stray spaces before the commas.
- Review: a report-only static review re-read about 90 of the page's 126 citation ranges in `raw/postgres-12/` at `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42` and reported 13 findings. The user then asked to fix all of them; this entry records the fixes. The page has no measurement script, so nothing was re-measured.
- Fixes on [the page](v12/questions/query-planning/plans-that-over-fetch-tuples.md):
  1. The statement that nothing re-reads stored statistics before `ANALYZE` is replaced by the one live correction: for inequality estimates, `ineq_histogram_selectivity()` re-reads the histogram ends from a non-partial B-tree index whose first column is the column (selfuncs.c:888-935, 5263-5310). This is fixed in the lead, the mental model, the F1 table, logic-map step [3] and the final summary.
  2. The F1 claim that a non-constant comparison value falls back to 0.005 for equality is split into two rows. With no statistics, equality uses `1 / n_distinct` (selfuncs.c:417-425, 5019-5116). Against a value unknown at plan time, it uses `var_eq_non_const()`, matching F10 (selfuncs.c:478-505). The fixed 0.005 applies only when `get_restriction_variable()` fails (selfuncs.c:258-262). The logic map is fixed too.
  3. The 10-page minimum now names its `!relhassubclass` condition and its firing on a table vacuumed while empty, and the "newly filled" wording is corrected (heapam_handler.c:2090-2118). This is fixed in the F1 table, the lifecycle table and the Evidence Map.
  4. The factor table's "stale visibility map" becomes "stale `relallvisible`". F5 now says the executor reads the visibility map live (nodeIndexonlyscan.c:161-163).
  5. F9 now limits the EXISTS/ANY fraction to sub-selects left as SubPlans, because pulled-up sub-selects become semi joins (prepjointree.c:168-186, subselect.c:1317). The 10% non-constant row is limited to statements with a `LIMIT` (planner.c:2876-2886, 2932).
  6. F10 adds the one-shot and no-parameter rules that precede the five-custom-plan rule (plancache.c:1016-1063).
  7. The F7 set-returning-function row adds the SQL-function inlining exception and its conditions (prepjointree.c:620-640, clauses.c:4911-4982, 5065-5074).
  8. The `allvisfrac` citations now cite the single-scan branch costsize.c:652-653 beside the repeated-scan branch 612-613, and the wording says pages, not fetches.
  9. The lossy-bitmap test row cites `set work_mem = 64` (bitmapops.sql:29-30) and adds a default/cause/mechanism note.
  10. The Bison claim also cites Makefile.global.in:741-744, and "Rows Removed by Filter" cites the scan-node case (explain.c:1632-1642).
  11. The `SET` and `RESET` statements in the EXPLAIN template carry the `wiki_overfetch_check` tag.
  12. The page now links the glossary entries HOT, VACUUM, Prepared statement, Partition pruning, Constraint exclusion, Autovacuum, and Semi join and anti join.
  13. The pseudoconstant example now follows the definition in pathnodes.h:1918-1926.
  - Smaller corrections: the F9 fraction denominator is the cheapest-total path's rows (planner.c:5896-5897), F1 lists "Six inputs", the heap-update citation starts at the old-page branch (heapam.c:3667-3681), and Context Reviewed, the Evidence Map and Source References gained the new sources. `## Contents` is unchanged because no heading changed.
- Glossary: [Semi join and anti join](glossary.md#semi-join-and-anti-join) gained a PostgreSQL 12 note and 12 in its checked-on list (nodes.h:702-719, prepjointree.c:168-186 and 2604-2631, analyzejoins.c:496-511, pathnode.c:1547-1560, nodeNestloop.c:214-230 and 318-322); all of these files were already in Source References. The Open Questions bullet on the 2026-09-26 entries now records the three of them checked on 12. Every other linked entry was reviewed for 12 scope; none needed a change.

## [2026-10-30] third review and fix v12 | Planner factors that produce plans that over-fetch tuples

- Prompt hygiene: review request "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , review question: # Planner Factors That Produce Plans That Over-Fetch Tuples in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)". The only correction drops the stray spaces before the commas; the meaning is unchanged. The user then chose "Fix and re-measure". The page has no measurement script, so the fixes were made and nothing could be re-measured.
- Review: a static review re-read all 161 cited ranges in `raw/postgres-12/` at `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`, plus the uncited `LIMIT` path in `grouping_planner()`, `relnode.c`, `create_limit_path()`, `tidbitmap.c`, the EXPLAIN join cases and `parsenodes.h`. It reported 8 findings, all fixed on [the page](v12/questions/query-planning/plans-that-over-fetch-tuples.md):

## [2026-10-30] eleventh review, fix and measure v12 | Planner factors that produce plans that over-fetch tuples

  1. Under row-level security, a two-sided range on a non-leakproof operator is estimated at 1 row. Each bound is `0.5 × (1 − null fraction)` (selfuncs.c:673-686), which `clauselist_selectivity()` does not recognize as the 1/3 default, so `hi + lo − 1 + null fraction` is 0 and becomes `1.0e-10` (clausesel.c:294-305, 315-332). F8's four-step list and a new three-row fallback table now show this, with `numeric_eq`, `numeric_lt` and `textlike` as non-leakproof examples (pg_proc.dat:152, 155, 1429, 1641, 4152, 4164). The F1 row was updated to match. F8 also gained the partitioned-table condition: child range-table entries get an empty `securityQuals` list (inherit.c:425-450), so partition statistics are visible only when the user can read the partition directly (selfuncs.c:4768-4838).
  2. F8's claim that the policy conditions reach the range-table entry as `securityQuals` now cites rewriteHandler.c:2043-2045 and 2097-2098.
- Measurement: new `### Measured example: row-level security and the estimates` in the Answer, and `## Measurement Script` with Usage, Last run, Stages, Non-default settings and the script (`of12-run.sh`, stages `build rls report stop clean`, sandbox `.wiki-runtime/tmp/of12/`, port 55447). It built 12.2 from the pin with default configure options and ran ten `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, TIMING OFF)` cases as the non-superuser role `alice` on 30,000-row tables, a size `ANALYZE` reads in full at the default statistics target, so the estimates are the same on every run. Results, estimated against actual rows: `numeric` `BETWEEN` 1000 against 1000 on the plain table and 1 against 1000 under row-level security; `n < 1000` 999 against 999 plain, 15000 against 999 under row-level security; `k = 0` 15000 against 15000 plain, 30 against 15000 under row-level security; the leakproof `int4` range 1000 on both; the partitioned table 2 against 1000 with `SELECT` on the parent only, 1001 after `SELECT` on the partitions. The server reported `proleakproof` true for `int4lt`, `int4ge` and `int4le`, and false for `numeric_eq`, `numeric_lt`, `numeric_ge` and `numeric_le`. Runs: 49 s from an empty sandbox, 3 s re-running `rls report`, then, after the script also recorded the `ge`/`le` flags, 47 s from an empty sandbox. The last two runs passed all 13 assertions with identical results, and the first run gave the same ten results. The published script is byte-identical to the one that ran last (checked with `diff` after extraction from the page). ## [2026-10-30] fourteenth review, fix and re-measure v12 | Planner factors that produce plans that over-fetch tuples

- Prompt hygiene: original wording "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , review question: # Planner Factors That Produce Plans That Over-Fetch Tuples in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)". Only the space before the comma was dropped; the meaning is unchanged. The review stated no scope, so it reported first. After the report, the user chose "Fix and re-measure".
- Review: a static re-read of the whole page against `raw/postgres-12/` at `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`. It reported 5 findings on [the page](v12/questions/query-planning/plans-that-over-fetch-tuples.md) and 1 glossary copy, all fixed:
  1. F9, its cursor note and the "Generated files" bullet said only SQL `DECLARE` sets `CURSOR_OPT_FAST_PLAN`. PL/pgSQL also sets it on every cursor declared in a block and every `OPEN` (pl_gram.y:585, 2071) and passes it to the planner through `SPI_prepare_params()` and the plan cache (pl_exec.c:2884-2885, 4575-4576, 4056-4059; spi.c:735, 2007; plancache.c:933). The F9 rows, the cursor note, the bullet (now also citing plpgsql/src/Makefile:73-76), the Evidence Map, Context Reviewed and Source References now say so.
  2. Contradiction: F1 and logic-map step [3] said the selectivities of several conditions on one table are multiplied, while F8's table combines a lower and an upper bound on one column as `hi + lo − 1`. The source supports F8 (clausesel.c:285-332). F1, the logic map (one new line), its key and the Evidence Map now name the range-pair exception.
  3. The live histogram-end correction was stated without its condition. `get_actual_variable_range()` runs only inside a histogram search, which needs a stored histogram with more than one entry that the operator may read (selfuncs.c:867-873). So the correction never happens under row-level security with a non-leakproof operator. The main idea, the mental model, the F1 row, the logic-map key, the final summary and the Evidence Map now carry the condition.
  4. F11's note said `enable_partition_pruning = off` only affects plan-time pruning. It also skips run-time pruning, because `create_append_plan()` and `create_merge_append_plan()` build the pruning information only while it is on (createplan.c:1211-1218, 1381-1383). The note, the settings-table row and the Evidence Map now say so.
  5. The script's `tag_sql()` used `\*` in a `sed` replacement, which POSIX leaves unspecified. It now uses `#` as the delimiter and writes `/*` and `*/` directly; the tagged output is unchanged.
- Measurement: the edited script was extracted from the page and run three times: a first untimed run from an empty sandbox; after `stop clean`, a run from an empty sandbox (61 s, `JOBS=22`); then `rls report` with the build present (4 s). Every run passed all 13 assertions, with the same ten results as every earlier run. `platform.txt` was unchanged. The Last run record and the Runtime row were updated.
- Glossary: the [Tuple fraction](glossary.md#tuple-fraction) PostgreSQL 12 note said "SQL `DECLARE` is what adds `CURSOR_OPT_FAST_PLAN`"; it now also names PL/pgSQL cursors, citing pl_gram.y:585, 2071 and pl_exec.c:4056-4059, and Source References gained `pl_gram.y` and `exec_prepare_plan()`. Leakproof function, Row-level security and Extended statistics were reviewed; no change. `verified_by_agent` stays `not yet`.
- Contradiction check: compared the whole page with itself, with the pinned source, with the linked glossary entries, with its index entries and versions.md note, and with the v12 partitioning and plan_cache_mode pages, then re-searched the page for every copy of each changed claim (`DECLARE`, cursor, multiplied/product, live correction, histogram end, all partitions). Found 1 contradiction (finding 2) and 4 incomplete or non-conforming statements, all fixed; the glossary copy of finding 1 was also fixed. Withdrawn: a candidate that merge-join estimates also read the live minimum and maximum, because that call is compiled out under `#ifdef NOT_USED` (selfuncs.c:5148-5151). 0 reported without a change.
- Bookkeeping: index entries and the versions.md note unchanged, because their summary still holds. Not committed.
- Isolation and teardown: the script's `rls` stage started and stopped one cluster under `.wiki-runtime/tmp/of12/data` on port 55447 in each run. The final `stop clean` found no `postmaster.pid`, no process naming the data directory, an empty socket directory and no listener, then deleted `.wiki-runtime/tmp/of12/` with its build; `pgrep -f` and `ss` confirmed it afterwards. The extracted script and console logs were removed. No subagent was used.

## [2026-10-30] review, fix and measure v12 | Query planner statistics sources

- Prompt hygiene: original wording "follow agents.md, in postgresql 12 , review question: # Query Planner Statistics Sources in PostgreSQL 12 (unverified)". Only the space before the comma was dropped; the meaning is unchanged. The review stated no scope, so it reported first. After the report, the user chose "Fix and measure".
- Review: a static re-read of [the page](v12/questions/query-planning/query-planner-statistics-sources.md) against `raw/postgres-12/` at `45b88269a353ad93744772791feb6d01bc7e1e42`, plus a check that every cited `file#Symbol` range contains its symbol (all did, or were sub-ranges inside the named function). It reported 12 findings, all fixed:
  1. Contradiction: the write-path paragraph said `ANALYZE` builds extended data, updates table and index `pg_class` rows, and reports to the collector, unconditionally. The inherited pass does none of these, and index rows are skipped under `VACUUM ANALYZE` (analyze.c:576-640). Replaced by a per-pass table.
  2. Contradiction: the inheritance-freshness paragraph said auto-analyze decides from the parent's own changes, without the condition that autovacuum never considers a partitioned table (autovacuum.c:2065-2067). Split into plain-inheritance and partitioned cases; measured.
  3. Contradiction: `verified_by_agent` carried a timestamp, and both index entries and the versions.md note said "fully reviewed", while items 1-2 were wrong. `verified_by_agent` is now `not yet`; the three summaries now say "reviewed, fixed and measured on 2026-10-30".
  4. Gap: extended statistics are built only from a plain-inheritance parent's own rows (analyze.c:576-584) but loaded for the whole-tree relation (plancat.c:430) and used by `estimate_multivariate_ndistinct()` without an `inh` check (selfuncs.c:3573-3585). New subsection with a comparison table, measured example and worked calculation. Whether a restriction-clause estimate on an appendrel parent also reaches it is filed under Open Questions.
  5. Gap: lazy `VACUUM` (vacuumlazy.c:341-363, 1803-1815) and every `index_build()` (index.c:2977-2986) also write `pg_class` size statistics.
  6. Wording: the histogram-endpoint probe was described as replacing a "stale" endpoint; it runs whenever the search touches an end entry. Also notes that the `get_variable_range()` probe is compiled out (selfuncs.c:5145-5151).
  7. Smaller gaps: the expression-index permission fallback to the parent (selfuncs.c:4636-4678), the `autovacuum_enabled` early return (autovacuum.c:3045-3051), and `initsplan.c#match_foreign_keys_to_quals` in the Evidence Map.
  8. No glossary links and no glossary link in Navigation; added term links on first use and the Navigation link.
  9. Explanation structure: added a mental-model value table, a branch table, a lifecycle-events table and a final causal summary.
  10. Non-default behavior: added the `postgres_fdw` `use_remote_estimate` default `false` (postgres_fdw.c:596-600), the hooks' `NULL` defaults and call order, the `join_hash.sql` hand-written `pg_class` fixture, and a defaults table for the auto-analyze conditions.
  11. Removed the revision-history sentence "The prior page made the index path too broad."
  12. Context Reviewed cited a regression run under `.wiki-runtime/pg12-build/` that no longer exists and had no script; it now says the page does not rely on it.
- Measurement: new `## Measurement Script` (`qs12-run.sh`, stages `build measure report stop clean`, sandbox `.wiki-runtime/tmp/qs12/`, port 55461). Extracted from the page and run from an empty sandbox (59 s, `JOBS=22`), then `measure report` re-run with the build present (11 s). Both passed all 15 assertions with identical results: whole-tree GROUP BY estimate 6000 before and 100 after the parent's ndistinct object (actual 10000); `ONLY` parent 3000 then 100 (actual 100); partitioned object never built; auto-analyze wrote 1 inherited row for the plain parent and none for the partitioned table. Measurement and source reading agree.
- Glossary: [pg_statistic](glossary.md#pg_statistic) gained a PostgreSQL 12 note with v12 citations (checked-on now 12, 17); [Extended statistics](glossary.md#extended-statistics) 12 note gained the whole-tree use of parent-only data; [Autovacuum](glossary.md#autovacuum) 12 note gained the relation-kind filter. The other linked entries were reviewed; no change. Glossary `verified_by_agent` stays `not yet`.
- Contradiction check: compared the assembled page with itself, the pinned source, the linked glossary entries, its index entries and versions.md note, and the v12 `plans-that-over-fetch-tuples` and `fk-join-optimization-two-tables` pages (searched for 10-page floor, defaults, `get_actual_variable_range`, partitioned auto-analyze, extended statistics on inheritance and partitioned tables, FK matching). Found 3 contradictions (findings 1-3), all fixed; none withdrawn; none reported without a change. No conflict on other pages.
- Common concepts: v12 has no common concept pages; none touched.
- Bookkeeping: updated the page's entries in `wiki/index.md` and `wiki/v12/index.md`, and its phrase in the v12 `wiki/versions.md` row. Not committed.
- Isolation and teardown: the `measure` stage started and stopped one cluster under `.wiki-runtime/tmp/qs12/data` on port 55461 in each run. The final `stop clean` found no `postmaster.pid`, no process naming the data directory, an empty socket directory and no listener, then deleted `.wiki-runtime/tmp/qs12/` with its build. The extracted script and run logs were deleted. Other clusters already running on the host were not started by this task and were left alone.
